Now, however, nothing in it rests upon an actual apprehension except that *this* man is not mortal; consequently this alone will also be decided, i.e. the *Omnis* will have to be furnished with an exception *for this one* (*excepto hoc* [this one excepted]). The *Nullus*, on the other hand, remains, insofar as the *Non-Hic* can also be a mere act of division of that which has already been multiplied in the *Nullus*; in other words, insofar as the A already posited in the *Nullus* can, on the occasion of an actual apprehension in a certain *Hic*, also have been merely repeated, in that I said:

*Non-Hic homo est mortalis*, [Non-this man is mortal]

[p. 63]

## Some further things that resulted from this test, connected with what was proved before.

1) Thinking, *as thinking*, has neither a difference of quality nor of quantity (A).

2.) The *possibility* of a difference of quality results only with the *object* as object (B, Non B).

3) The *quality itself*, however, can only be determined through apprehensions (C).

4) An object as object has no (logical) quantity.

5) Although an object as object has of itself no (logical) quantity: yet it grounds, after the preceding C, the *possibility* of a difference of quantity.

6) The *quantity itself*, however, can only be determined through apprehensions (= C).

7) An *Omne*, like a *Nullum*, is merely a *Not-some*, consequently says merely the most complete complexion of apprehensions made, such as can take place in men, as already accomplished in a certain case. Hence the one, like the other, designates the greatest section of human imagining-into the world.

[p. 64] So much follows from *what has been said hitherto* for the *Omne* and *Nullum*.

8) For *concepts* in general, *as concepts* (not as thoughts) there follows further (but, as said) *merely from what has been said hitherto*;—for the row where *they* may stand above comes only afterwards,—that a concept as concept, *as one has hitherto understood it*, is a mere complexion of sensible apprehensions.

*Rem.* How may it have come about that for the origin of concepts as concepts one has never yet had a *law of thought* of its own? The leap from the laws of thought to the origin of concepts, without finding in the laws of thought, *as laws of thought*, anything laid down in preparation for it, was indeed striking.—The *Kantians* remedied this inconvenience excellently by showing the origin of concepts only after the concepts. This passed for *pure*; for the impure was finally carried along only as a mere *by-the-way* of pure logic.—Through *concepts*, it should seem to one, merely *nature* constructs itself *in us*, as, according to preceding apprehensions of *our genus*, it constructs itself outside us. For we have, after all, no concepts [p. 65] of it until it constructs itself; and it does, after all, construct itself in the most finely organized heads, which for that reason are called *acute*, also in the manner most conformable to its actual course, still far from any preceding *Why*, indeed a thousand times, if the *Why* is to be forced in after the fact, under a false *prius*; hence perhaps the poets, like the philosophers, sometimes *prophesy* so happily, and most happily when they decline the *Why* in the process. *Kant* noticed this, and did so; for he declared logic (i.e. thinking of a *Why*, particularly after preceding *Critique*) closed since *Aristotle*, according to the Preface to the second edition of the *Critique of Pure Reason*, pp. VIII, IX. The whole remark, brought in here under No. 8, is to count, like my hitherto heaped-up parentheses, for no more than these too; for it lets one only *surmise*; but *surmised is not thought*. One must also occasionally rest, i.e. in the midst of thinking not refuse the request of one’s craving *to want to know* something provisionally.

9) A *concept* is, *as that which, as concept, it has hitherto in all logics shown itself to be,* [p. 66] perfectly just that which an apprehension is; and apprehensions, *without concepts*, (in the animals) can lead to just that to which one (in us men) attains also through concepts. Concepts, on the other hand, without apprehensions, would, *as no concepts*, never be able to lead to that to which one attains through apprehensions.

10) An *Omne* (say in the major of a syllogism) prefixed to certain concepts, as *indispensable* security of the universality of their assertion, is an *Omne* prefixed to certain apprehensions. (Just so the *Nullum*).

11) An *Omne* which, as *indispensable* security of the universality of their assertion (say in the major of a syllogism), were prefixed to certain concepts *without apprehensions*, would be prefixed to apprehensions and also to *no* apprehensions, consequently a contradiction. (Just so the *Nullum*).

12) There is for a syllogism consisting of concepts, hence also for that of which it materially consists, for concepts, *as that which they have meanwhile shown themselves to be in all logics*, for judgments, *as judgments* (without up to now bringing the *A* into account) [p. 67] no other truth than the correctness of the apprehensions on which they rest, hence *the truth of their materiature*.

## Necessary Anticipation of Some Propositions from the Doctrine of Primal Division (Ur-theilung) or of the Object, for the Purpose of the Next Following 15th §.

1) Thinking, *as thinking*, must annihilate matter, *as matter* in thinking; otherwise it does not arrive, with matter, at a *something, as something thought*.

*Rem. Spoken as well as written* words, e.g., must be annihilated *as matter* in hearing and reading, if thoughts are to arise from them.

2) But as the *being-thought* in the case of a matter presupposes an annihilation of it *as matter*, just as well does the fact that one *can* get a thought from it presuppose something *in* this matter which absolutely cannot be annihilated by thinking, in a thinking. Without the former there would arise from it no *thought*, without the latter there would arise from it nothing *thought*, but rather a thing thought and also nothing thought, i.e. a contradiction.

[p. 68] 3) If it is therefore, *after preceding apprehensions* (= C), to come with the matter in thinking, through thinking, to a *something, as something thought*: then this process of thinking, applied to a matter, *must* (by virtue of No. 2) bring with it a *primal division* (*Ur-theilung*) in what is thought

a) as a *something thought* (B *actuality*)

b) as *thought* (Non-B *possibility*.)

4) In other words, this means: thinking procures for itself, through its application to matter, an object = B, Non-B, or, as I can now express it more correctly, after the underlaying of the thoughts themselves which it designates, without having to fear misunderstandings any more,

B (minus) − B | |

*Actuality Possibility* as thought as thought.

Together

| *Object*

as a *something in a thinking*, as *representation*, to be sure after the lack of a thinking on it has been removed, hence no longer *mere* representation, [p. 69] but also without any other plus than is essentially required for the possibility of the thinking of a something.

This the above twofold, over which A, *after it has got hold of a material C*, plunges into life. These the two rows of the *primal division* (*Ur-theilung*), in which *thinking*, eternally equal to itself and just the selfsame, flows out over everything in its application (in us men). All our cognizing, every concept, every judgment, the categorical as well as the hypothetical, every syllogism rests therefore upon a preceding disjunction, as the first thing in the application of a thinking. This logical adventurousness may please or not; *it must* be so. (An imperative that has nothing to fear from any *imperativo caret* [it lacks an imperative], like *Kant’s* synthetic knots).

5) This object, as *object*, is the beginning not of thinking itself,—thinking itself neither begins nor ends,—but of the *application* of a thinking to matter, is pure thought lying at the ground of all cognizing, in which everything apprehensible is annihilated in a thinking through thinking as far as it could be annihilated, a) in order not to become a thing thought and also Nothing thought, i.e. a [p. 70] contradiction, and thus b) to let apprehensions, in so far as they previously went along as a Not-thinking, as *mere* representations, *now under a thinking* attach themselves once more to themselves (*an sich*), as to a something that has now, in thinking, through thinking, become indestructible.

*Rem.* This something of an object in itself (*an sich*), which, after preceding apprehensions, has become indestructible in thinking through thinking, has beguiled *Kant*, when he lets a thing in itself (call it *Idea*, or what he will) be posited by *pure reason* itself, i.e. by thinking, as a *mere* thinking, when he further lets *pure reason*, as pure, hence *thinking as a thinking*, press upon an absolute totality. Not to mention that he at once debases again to downright contradiction the thought of an *absolute* (see above), stirred up in him so promisingly, through the addition of a totality; for a *totum* is a *quantum*, and a *quantum* cancels being-*absolute*; not to mention this, then, a thinking which, as thinking, pressed upon a totality of itself, would, upon a limitation of itself, i.e. upon a termination of itself, i.e. upon A as A and upon [p. 71] A as Non-A, i.e. upon a contradiction, press.

6) Should then *this* something still be a something, it must still distinguish itself from thinking itself as *mere* thinking.

7) What now distinguishes itself from thinking, *as thinking*, only so far as is necessary in order to be still thought as a something, is necessary for a thinking *in its application*.

8) Necessary is, in order to be still thought as a something, that on the not-thought, hence *on* the matter in the *mere* representation, something adhere that can in no way be annihilated in thinking through thinking (No. 2).

9) Necessary, therefore, to the end that on the still not-thought, hence *on* the matter in the *mere* representation, an equally unalterable *modus* of *being-represented* adhere, as on thinking *one* unalterable *modus generalis* and two unalterable *modi speciales* of thinking adhere; (those of thinking are A as A 1) in A; A as A 2) *through* A).

10) What is necessary shows itself here also actually. The unalterable *modus generalis* [p. 72] of *being-represented* of something represented is that it is not, just as A is thought already *as* A, equally, as that which it is, given wholly and completely already with the fact that it is, but rather is represented *outside one another*. This the *modus generalis* of being-represented, just as conversely A already as A is *modus generalis* of thinking. The former is necessary, because otherwise there would be no something, as *distinct* something for thinking, i.e. no object, as it has hitherto been understood everywhere *logically*.

11) That for the possibility of an object as object a being-represented, as a being-represented *outside-one-another*, is essentially required, hence that no A already *as* A can take place here, *as* in a mere representation, so much, methinks, is clear.

12) Clear, consequently, also, that an object without an *outside-one-another* in the representation would be no object;

13) Clear, consequently, also, that an object assumed without an *outside-one-another* in the representation, as hitherto everywhere, would be an object and also no object, consequently a contradiction.

14) A mere witticism, hence for thinking as thinking an absurdity, which it, for the sake of diversion, [p. 73] pardons, would it be to want to split in two this *outside-one-another*, which thinking itself inevitably demands for the possibility of an object for it, perhaps still *ad analogiam modorum specialium* of thinking, were there not problems which made this splitting necessary for thinking itself in its *application*.

15) The problems which are at issue here demand 1) that that *outside-one-another*-being-represented found essential to the thinking of an object be broken down into a being-represented *one after another*, i.e. into occurring *alterations* for the sake of the possibility of a *representation* in general (for this already includes an alteration) and of a *distinct* something in thinking, but in particular for the sake of the thought of *a time*, in which thought thinking itself alone, applied to a *succession* in the representations, then describes itself. In this *succession* namely the very opposite of the A as A already thought in A is found; consequently A can be posited as in a *something*, therein; let this something now be whatever it will. (See below the *test*).

[p. 74] *Rem. Time* is a thought. The *succession*, or an occurring alteration, is a necessary condition of the possibility of a representation in general, hence also of the applicability of a (human) thinking in particular. *Time*, by contrast, is thinking itself, already applied to the *succession* in the representations, or A infinitely repeatable as A in the *succession* of the representations. *Kant* concluded thus here: because time is no concept, and yet is necessary, it must be only something wholly new, an *a priori pure intuition*. The syllogism is built immediately on the (critically, hence not, as otherwise, applied) *principium exclusi tertii*; for thoughts there are in the *Critique* only at the back; hence at the front and in the *Transcendental Aesthetic* only the choice stood open between *concepts* (as, in my opinion, seeing) and *pure* (as *blind*) intuitions. A *tertium* would have been a contradiction. I refrain from going further, and here from the *Transcendental Aesthetic* add only, after pp. 47 and 71, some —

[p. 75]

## Words of the Critique.

“The representation which can be given only through *a single object given* (it is a question of the representation of *time*) is however intuition? — Nor would the proposition that different times cannot be simultaneous be derivable from a universal concept; (hit!) — The proposition is *synthetic*, and cannot arise from concepts *alone*.” (Thus *pure* intuition and yet a *representation* given through an *object*, not to forget the *single one*, and this *time*; — Thus a *synthetic* proposition, that one cannot think a *being-one-after-another* as a *being-simultaneous*, hence no contradiction; — Thus a synthetic proposition which indeed can arise from concepts, but only not from concepts *alone*? — Yet, as should already be apparent from this, and as stands in the *Critique* p. 71 *in so many words*, thinking itself surely has limits *at all times*).

## Words of the Critique.

It is here a question of the object of natural theology, hence of *God*, who, without the conditions of time and space, as mere *pure* intuitions *of man and other finite beings*, is in turn [p. 76] supposed to have his *own pure* intuition; “for,” it says, “all his cognition *must* be of this kind, and not *thinking*, *which proves limits at all times*”— Enough, more than enough!—

Whether it is true that since these words of the *Critique* one in Germany could now also make one’s own time for oneself, I leave undecided.

16) The problems which are at issue here demand further 2) that that *outside-one-another*-being-represented found essential to the thinking of an object be broken down into a being-represented *beside one another*, as the second *modus specialis* of that *modus generalis* of a being-represented outside-one-another, found essential to the thinking of an object. An object, then, if it is to be an object for a (human) thinking, may not, just as A is already thought *through* A, be represented as one *through* the other, but already as object includes a *being-represented-beside-one-another*. This is, through the cognized necessity of an *outside-one-another* for every possible object of a (human) thinking, basically already conceded, and opens up on that first one merely the second [p. 77] How *(modus specialis) in accordance with the problems at hand*. But since it, notwithstanding its suitability for the solution of those problems, hence notwithstanding its serviceability as a *hypothesis*, may nevertheless meet with resistance as a thesis: I will, superfluously, seek to prove it too as a *thesis*, in the hope that this investigation also, if its standpoint is right, can only *gain* by going deeper. *Proved* I then call it, when an object, *without a being-beside-one-another in a (human) thinking represented*, for this thinking comes out upon a representation and also upon no representation in thinking, hence upon a contradiction, indisputably.

*Proof that, if an object without a being-beside-one-another in a (human) thinking were to be represented, this object, thus represented, would come out upon a representation and also upon no representation in thinking, hence upon a contradiction, indisputably.*

A *representation* designates either the *act* of representing in a thinking, [p. 78] or that which this act brings along with it for thinking, *the represented itself*. That act includes a *succession*, this is proved; for otherwise it would be no *distinct* something, no act.

That which this act brings along with it for thinking, *the represented itself*, is however either a represented or no represented. No represented in a thinking, and yet thought as a represented in a thinking, is a contradiction. That (a represented in a thinking, namely) it cannot be, however, without essentially distinguishing itself from thinking; for otherwise it would be a represented and also no represented, hence again a contradiction. So in order not to be a represented in a thinking and also no represented in a thinking, the represented must essentially distinguish itself from thinking. So much I bring out indisputably through mere thinking over against a *representing, as representing*. But the *How* of this essentially requisite difference with regard to the *nature of the represented itself*, (not with regard to the essentially requisite *act* of a *being-represented* in general) also still from *thinking as thinking*, *representing as representing*, *wholly alone*, to want to determine [p. 79] would likewise be a contradiction; for it is now a question of the *How* of a *represented as represented*, of which *How* I, through mere combinations from a *thinking and representing* in general, can impossibly bring out anything other than that the *How* of this represented must be essentially different from the *How* of thinking. Herewith I would then, with my further proof, for the *essential* constitution of this *How* of a represented, whose *difference* from the *How* of a thinking I have nonetheless cognized as *essentially necessary*, be referred to that which I shall never be able to think as *non-nature* on the *nature* of this represented itself, hence shall *have to* think as the essential constitution of this *How* of a represented. Only in this way can I become wise about what my thought of an *outside-one-another*, which in *me* already necessarily included the thought of a *beside-one-another*, actually meant to demonstrate with this *beside-one-another*. I take the path, sure that it must lead to the goal.

The material I call C. Without this C no application of a (human) thinking is, at the awakening of the same in a human individual, [p. 80] possible; for upon what is it otherwise to be applied, at its awakening in a human individual? Upon this material A annihilates in thinking whatever can be annihilated in it *as material*; otherwise material and thought, hence what was previously diametrically opposed, would *wholly become one*, i.e. a non-thing, a contradiction, or else nothing at all would come to be thought (as something *represented* in thinking). I say, what was previously diametrically opposed would either wholly become one, or else nothing thought (as a something *represented* in thinking) would come of it, if the *material as material* were not annihilated in thinking, *as thinking*. What was then in this case diametrically opposed to one another? *Material and thinking*, in other words *material and form*. If it must now however be conceded that to that material in its *mere* representation, as to something not yet thought, a certain *How* of outside-one-anotherness, which my *beside-one-anotherness*, already taken up into this *outside-one-anotherness*, now fully of itself determined perfectly through really present *dimensions of space*, inalterably adheres: then it must also be conceded that this material itself has an *unalterable form*. One form, *as form*, cannot annihilate [p. 81] the other; consequently thinking too, *as form*, cannot annihilate that which adheres to the material *as form*; (this would be, again, to annihilate a *copula*.) If thinking cannot annihilate that which is form in the material, and if the mentioned beside-one-anotherness, *now determined through dimensions of space*, is manifestly its form also outside a (human) thinking: (for how else would it go with the animals?) then this *beside-one-anotherness according to dimensions of space* also in the thinking of an *object in general*, in which nonetheless all material, *as material*, is annihilated, cannot be annihilated without an annihilation of form *through a form*, i.e. without a contradiction. But, it will be said, *consequently also not be thought*. Correct; this form *can* not be thought, but rather *must* be thought. That in which no material is any longer to be annihilated by a thinking in thinking, and which is nonetheless still a something, must be thought.—If at my table here everything that is material in it is annihilated by my thinking in thinking: then it has thereby gained; for it can now, as that which it has become in me,—*as thought*,—no longer be annihilated, but rather *must* be thought, (*Formæ rerum æternæ* [the eternal forms of things]).

[p. 82]

### Only some of the corollaries which result from this proof.

α) Without a *beside-one-another according to dimensions of space* in its representation, an object would be something represented and also nothing represented, hence a contradiction (so long as a *human* thinking in its application proceeds only from C, hence from a material).

β) Space is, as *extension*, the essentially necessary condition of the representability of an object, as of a something still to be represented in thinking. Space is, *according to its three dimensions*, the beside-one-another *indestructible* in thinking by thinking, hence the *form* on the material, as a something still to be represented in thinking. Space is, *according to the detail of its geometrical relations* (that is, drawn into *reckoning*) an application of thinking as a thinking to the beside-one-another *indestructible* in thinking by thinking, hence to the *form* on the material, as a something still to be represented in thinking. This the ground of the *apodictic* in *geometry*.

[p. 83] *Rem.* This apodictic quality in a *beside-one-another* has perhaps helped *Kant* to the discovery of his *a priori* synthetic propositions; whereby he then shoved upon thinking itself that which lies in the form of the *material*, namely the copula of the *material*, the synthesis of an indestructible *beside-one-another*, without being A itself; consequently he assumed in his *a priori* synthetic propositions, as propositions which are supposed to lie already *synthetically* in the *essence of thinking* itself, an A, where, *as in a synthesis*, or copula of the *material*, no A lies, but merely an essential condition of the applicability of an A to a material, in order to gain for itself a *something* in it. In short, the copula of *matter*, in order to be represented as a something in a thinking, concresced for him in his phantasy with the copula of *thinking*, which is merely A infinitely many times repeatable as A *in* A and *through* A; he took into the A a non-A as A; thus he floundered, a whole Critique through, in sheer contradictions; for the *prius* was an *error*.

γ) An *occurring alteration* (a *succession*) is an essential condition [p. 84] of the possibility of a representation in general as of an *act* (as of an *actus*). *Extension* (a *beside-one-another*) is an essential condition, if this act is to *bring something with it* for thinking, consequently essential condition of the *represented* itself, as of a something *still to be represented* in thinking.

δ) No *object* can be represented in a thinking without *extension*. My table there, my pen, my paper, in the thought of them, represented without extension, would be a table and not-table, etc.—As it is with individuals, so it is also with genera, *as that which they have hitherto shown in the logics*, with species, subspecies, so long as a human thinking in its application proceeds only from C, hence from a material.

### Addendum for those to whom this consequence might seem too hard.

A, combined with C, and with this producing a (B−−B), in order thereby to be able to take up the C again into a representation for a thinking, this is the circle which our thinking describes in its application. But tell me now, how it would be possible [p. 85] to take up the C again in (B−−B) into a representation for a thinking, if in that (B−B) even the essential form of this (C) had been annihilated through thinking (A)?—No, under the persistence of this indestructible form of *beside-one-anotherness*, rather, our whole *actual imagining-into* the world, and, what is often still dearer to us,—our *phantasy* finds its place; just as, through the persistent in the *succession*, a *memory* finds itself assigned a day’s work, to be carried on in us in the service of a thinking. One should therefore do it as a favor to one’s phantasy, and consider whether each of its images, already *as image*, must not contain something spatial, or else be an image and also no image? But one should also do healthy common sense a small favor, (I speak with the critical arrogance) and consider whether the wholly space- and timeless *Kantian* categories, as *isolated* forms of the possibility of a thinking, are a thing and also no thing, or not? In children, who begin to think, they would be a thing, since there everything still goes by *images* (*general-images*, as some call them): but then they are no [p. 86] *Kantian* categories; in us older people they would become *Kantian* categories: but would begin, by my modest estimation, precisely thereby, to become, in us older people, a thing and also no thing; thinking would slip away from one, the older one grew, out of sheer purity, under thinking, in that its object lost all extension through twelve categories. It is also truly noteworthy that *Kant* need only attach pure syllogisms to his categories in order to produce from them, in the business of pure inferring, hence, as he believed, *through a wholly pure thinking*, *ideas*. Were his categories so pure, so wholly space- and timeless as he thinks, he would have been able to let nothing arise through them beyond the bounds of a possible apprehension, not even ideas any more, but would have been compelled to take the right path here, and to take refuge in *phantasy* in this case. What finally is actually thought in the *Kantian* categories is *eo ipso* no longer a *Kantian* category, but a *thinking*, as *this*, however, it cannot be shredded into *isolated* forms as conditions of its possibility. Rather it is, where it is, one and the selfsame.

[p. 87] 17) I believe, through what was said under No. 16, and in particular through No. γ, to have shown that I fear no consequence, as soon as it forces itself inescapably upon one after an incontrovertible (that is, just *actual*) *prius* (that is, is just a *consequence*). The word: *Some*, added to the corollaries in the heading before No. α), means merely that I wish to withhold here *in detail* the elucidation which that preceding proof gives to *mathematicians* about the *ground* of the incontrovertibility of their science, to *psychologists* about the whole business of phantasy in man, because it did not belong here.—But how could I forget that, indeed, awakened through C, effective in C through the production of a B·B out of the same, my own thinking, where it has broken through to the highest *possible* purification from that C as from this B−B, *and been turned back only into itself, can also think only itself*? Its form too is indestructible, because it is form; and its form is no outside-one-another, no after-one-another, no *beside-one-another*, hence apodictically certain *no extension*, but apodictically certain *space- and timeless*. In its purely thought laws (A as A in A, through A) I think to myself indeed nothing other than my thinking itself, [p. 88] and yet think. Yes, already in each −B, i.e. in every possibility, as possibility, in each thought *as thought* without a represented *something*, hence without a representation, hence without an *object*, as it has hitherto been determined, I think nothing other than my thinking itself, and yet think.

Be greeted by me, holy Light, I might exclaim here with *Milton*; if every light were not only a reflection of this Light, *after a material has stepped in between*.

18) Even if this −B is no object, as an object has hitherto been described, and as it, as object, as representation in a thinking, as a *represented* something in a thinking, must be: I should nonetheless have to annihilate through thinking in thinking a form, would have to annihilate precisely the form which concerns me most nearly, if I did not ascribe to it reality, or,—how shall I, hovering again over this abyss, name it?—the purest *Being* (εἶναι). But this matter is far too important for me not also to seek out, for this ground of its assertion, *already unshakable in itself* (so long as no form as form can be annihilated through a form as form), [p. 89] everything that might serve its further illumination.

19) I do so, then, and now, disregarding B, pursue that −B further. To be sure I arrive, and this cannot be otherwise, on this path at sheer minuses; at the minus of a representation, at the minus of a something thought in a *representation*, at the minus of an object, at the minus of an outside-one-another, at the minus of a beside-one-another; consequently also at the minus of a time and at the minus of a space; consequently also at the minus of an (animal) life, and at the minus of an existence *which would admit a possible being-there, and a possible being-otherwise than what it is*.

20) But, notwithstanding all these minuses which follow from it, I could, without this −B (this possibility), also have no B (no actuality) as a *thought* in me; just as, without B as a thought, I should also *know* nothing of −B. The −B thus grounds the possibility of B as *a thought*; the B on the other hand points only to something that I have already *learned to think* through the lead of −B. Can then the −B, and what it brings with it in its train, be a *nothing*?

[p. 90] 21) Disregarding B, and going back still further to this −B, I come finally, notwithstanding all the minuses following from it, even upon laws of its own, which this −B has. I therefore find myself here surely not in the domain of nothing; however much I remain aware thereby that I have now been carried away above everything extended and for that reason representable, above space and time, above everything visible and palpable. *These laws are.* Were they not, how could I have to thank them for everything that is *thought* in my knowing and cognizing? What in my knowing and cognizing is merely *thought*, is, *as thought, necessary*. Necessary, then, are these laws. They are eternally the same, are true and capable of neither restriction nor exception. Eternally the same, true, capable of neither restriction nor exception, are these laws; their not-thinking, like their thinking-otherwise, would be a contradiction. Purified so far that language can still express them, they run: *A as A, in A, through A.*

22) The B on the object is accordingly now so led into my thinking that it points down the row to *representations*, and (under *this* point of view) in favor of the *representability* of a *something* (*ad formam* [p. 91] C), cannot be annihilated. The −B is now so led into my thinking that it (under *this* point of view) no longer, as downward in the row *everywhere*, needing a B (*ad formam* C), now also upward in the same points to *thoughts as thoughts*, and in favor of a *thinking, as thinking* (*ad formam* A, if it has been awakened through C), cannot be annihilated. Now, however, in the application of a thinking downward from thinking itself, and into the physical, no −B can be *represented* in thinking without a preceding B, (notwithstanding that the possibility is presupposed *in thinking* along with the actuality), and only B−B *together* give *here* an object, (proved above); hence I can also speak only *here* of an object (an existence, being-there under a thinking); but in thinking itself no longer of an object (an existence, being-there under a thinking); rather I must there speak of a *Being* simply.

23) And if I then speak there of Being simply, of what kind of *Being* do I speak, according to what has gone before? Is it a *dialectical Being*, with which my own thinking, my *own pure reason*, puts me off and deceives, indeed in its ground and essence [p. 92] —as with a hereditary-sinful indestructible lie, on which it was caught only some thirty years ago—must put off and deceive? This, since the memory of man, only *one* man has so asserted, in mockery of reason, as it is now asserted in Germany, and thousands have parroted it. No, it is a true, necessary, eternal and unchangeable, of no exception or restriction capable, *Being*, whose not-thinking or thinking-otherwise than it is would be a contradiction; it is a *Being* such as philosophy, before its critical execution, from the times of *Pythagoras* and of *Plato* onward ever considered, as the Being of an *intelligible* world, which reveals itself in the thinking of man, and as a Being, *as it is with God*, and about which it, as apodictic certainty of a Being outside this changeable existence in material, was glad in its most thinking advocates.

How things have gone with metaphysics, with the doctrine of God, toward the end of the 18th century in Germany, a note to No. 23.

A philosopher who wanted to master the whole of philosophy rose up, behind a mighty [p. 93] bulwark of new words, with the dubious exception against the thesis of a God:

*“Whether then the subjective thought of a God might also have objective reality?”*

That, he meant, the simple-minded schools before him had never yet thought of, and that was surely the main thing. Now it rang from mouth to mouth: in philosophy, whose pulse the right physician had only just felt, a quite singular crisis was manifesting itself, about which one must take counsel, collectively and individually. Said, done! Whoever did not join in had no head.—They were consequently nothing but geniuses; and whoever was not yet one knew how to remedy, through an all-positing *I*, the stinginess of nature in an incomprehensible brevity of time,—as even the most esteemed learned journals of Germany could not conceal,—in the happiest way. Suddenly he rose, and became, after a little critical evolution-sickness,—a universal genius. If now, however, a non-universal-genius might permit himself, merely in all the simplicity of the simple old schools, a question to the gentlemen, from the head of the sect on down, about the point set forth above, which has gradually become their favorite matter! It would be: whether [p. 94] the famous *Königsberg man* also knew what he was saying when he could ask after an *objective reality* in God, i.e. after a reality falling within space and time, extended, belonging to material, either, were it possible, quite dead, or at most animal, palpable, and hence *never purely intelligible*,—*and that in God*, as an *intelligence* wholly pure in the eyes of every rational being (in German: a *spirit*)?—The harm which this man has already done through such an immature objection, which has no sense and can have none, is plain to see.[^94-1] But so it goes, [p. 95] when one wants to write a critique of thinking, and does not know either what *thinking* is, or what an object is. Had *Fichte* been able to wind himself out again from his *I*, into which *Kant*, as a consistent head, had to entangle him at last, and which, let Fichte determine it as he will, still always remains an *individuality*: *this* reproach he would have given to the founder of his sect *himself*. He was the one deceived through *Kant’s* fault. His acuteness wanted to go forward on the ground that had been laid; the ground was good for nothing, and he fell. If the wagon falls, says a provincial proverb among us, it has always had five wheels, i.e. the grossest faults, *which no man wanted to notice in it before*. I pity this man from the bottom of my heart, let him make of *me* what he will; *with Kant*, on the other hand, I am vexed; and if I do *him* an injustice: then let it be protested; I will answer for it. *Dixi!* [I have spoken!] —

[^94-1]: That he himself did not know what he wanted in this is evident also from the fact that he was able, after the event, to invest the idea of a God—rejected in the *Critique of Pure Reason* as *constitutive*, i.e. as something that asserted an actual being—with the significance of a *regulative for our empirical cognitions*. For according to the constitutively dialectical idea of a God, man is nevertheless supposed to regulate his experiences, “in that one (these are words of the *Critique*, p. 698) derives the object of experience, *as it were*, from the imagined object of this idea, as from its ground or cause.” So

a *vel quasi*-deriving [an “or as if” deriving] of the objects of experience from a, *constitutively* dialectical nevertheless, God, as if he were their cause, makes God into a regulative, and makes *eo ipso* [by that very fact] this regulative into a nonentity, because it is *constitutively* dialectical.

[p. 96] 24) What can still be thought α) outside *space* alone, and, because we think, *must* be thought, is thinking itself in its *being*, not in a *spatial* something, together with that to which it points according to the last numbers.

25) What can still be thought β) outside *time* alone, and, because we think, *must* be thought, is thinking itself in its *being*, not in a *temporal* something, together with that to which it points according to the last numbers.

*Rem. Hence*, (α, β), the striking contrast of one infinity (of *being* as of a *thinking*), outside another infinity (of space and time, upon which one then nevertheless again imposed limits against that infinity)—in moments of consecration in sublime spirits, and nowhere more sublime than in the great *Haller*, *on eternity*, when he says of God:

The swift pinions of thoughts, Against which time, and sound, and wind, And even the wing of light are slow, Grow weary over thee, and hope for no limits; I heap up monstrous numbers, [p. 97] Mountains of millions upon millions, I roll time upon time, and world upon world into a heap; And when from the fearful height, Dizzy, I look back again toward thee, All the power of number, multiplied a thousand times, Is not yet a part of thee; *I annihilate it, and thou liest whole* *before me.*

*Hence*, however, also the *representationless feeling* in moments of consecration in religiosity generally!—

26) No matter can be *thought* as matter, but can, as matter, be represented only for a thinking (in order to make an object out of it *for itself*).

27) What, in matter, is *form* (*copula materiae* [copula of matter]) can not only, but *must* be thought, i.e. it *must* adhere indestructibly to the representation of C in a thinking, hence also to every object (B−B *downward* in the series and into physical life). If it *must* be thought in *this* way, which could only now be developed comprehensibly: then it *must* also be represented in thinking through something that is neither *itself* a [p. 98] thinking, nor can, as that which it is, ever *itself* become a thinking (through annihilation of form); for otherwise *forms* of matter (an outside-one-another, a succession, a juxtaposition, and the last according to three dimensions) would already adhere to thinking *as a thinking*, which is absurd; (A at cross purposes, hence Non-A).

*Rem.* Here, at this being-absurd, something occurs to me about a *Critical Idealism* which I find indicated here and there as a still further spun-out *Kantian-Fichtean* philosophy. Should this Critical Idealism perhaps likewise consist in this (for I have never inquired more closely after it)—that *space* too, like time, would already be thought as positable through the I: then this I is either the thinking = A, or the object = B−B, or the material = C. Is it the thinking, it is an absurd I according to No. 27; is it the object, it is no I of the newest stamp: is it the material, it is again no I; consequently this new Critical Idealism, presupposed (as I must conclude, if the gentlemen are consistent) that it still turns about an I, would be an excrescence, proceeding from the Critical philosophy, of the fact that [p. 99] *Kant*, (be it practical or theoretical) lets, through thinking as thinking, a something (hence an object, hence space and time) already be posited *in man*; and so had to drive his adherents out at last upon *apriorist visions*, as the no longer communicable ground of all and every philosophy. To be sure, one might think that the sight of the animals ought already to have convinced these newest sages of the world that the being-represented *in juxtaposition and succession* cannot lie in the *human-I* alone, but must already adhere to consciousness in an animal life, hence also to the *horse-I* etc., as I, to my astonishment, have just now noticed in one of them, if representations are to come about in it. But this conviction would have been of *empirical* and not, as must be the case in a true philosophy, of *intuitive-transcendental* origin. Away with it!

28) What in matter is *form must* therefore be thought *in the indicated sense*. But now the question arises whether, then, apart from *succession* and *juxtaposition*, nothing in matter is *form*, hence apart from this nothing [p. 100] in it *must* be thought *in the indicated sense of the word*?

29) If A is once posited in a certain C through B−B: then in this C everything *that can be thought* in it (as object) runs back to B−B. What accordingly in C cannot be brought back to B−B (as object) is not thought in C (as object). But what in it runs back to B−B *as this*, that *must* be thought in C (as object), and is hence *form* of C, (as of an *object*). So much for now; the rest in the doctrine of *primal division* (*Ur-theilung*) or *doctrine of the object* itself!—And in order to be able to devote ourselves to this then (in the further development of logic) undividedly, we must here first rid ourselves entirely of *time*.

30) *Time* draws itself, namely, in the property of No. 25, parallel with space (No. 24); this is not to be denied. But from another side, as the condition of the possibility of *inner* as well as of outer apprehensions, *as one now says*, whereas space is a condition of their possibility only for *outer* apprehensions, it threatens new difficulties. They are not dangerous. What there is in them, the test will show.

[p. 101]

## Test of the Propositions Anticipated Here from the Doctrine of the Object (from No. 1–30).

The contribution which these numbers will unfailingly supply to the explanation of what I still have to say about logic, combined with the marks of consistency which they, as it seems to me, bear in themselves, is without doubt the most proven test of their correctness. To forestall misunderstandings, and to let what in them is still *capable* or in need of a more precise determination receive this determination in such a manner that only arbitrariness (a not-thinking) could dispute the truth of the asserted propositions, this is therefore alone what must still be accomplished *this* time under *this* heading.

From matter, it says in No. 2, there would come to be nothing thought, but rather something thought and also Nothing-thought, unless something adhered to it which could not be annihilated through thinking in a thinking. To whom this proposition, put in this way, does not want to be evident, he finds it under another position in the proof to No. 16, where it reads thus: in this material A annihilates in thinking what in it can be annihilated as *material*; otherwise [p. 102] in thinking material and thought, hence what was previously diametrically opposed, would become *wholly one*, i.e. a nonentity, a contradiction, or else there would come to be no thought at all. Note that here, to be sure, both times there is talk of the thinking of a *matter*, hence of the *representing* of a matter in a thinking; yet both times the word is used: *Thought*: not: *Represented*. Why this? Is not the thinking of a matter, as *thinking* of a matter, a contradiction, if it must, in order to be able to be thought, be annihilated in thinking through thinking as *matter*? Certainly, a downright contradiction! Why then, nevertheless, the expressions that include a contradiction, such as this one: *from matter there would come nothing thought*? Answer α) as to the contradiction, may one be pleased to attend to this, that it is *not* said *the* matter, but *from* the matter; and the contradiction is gone. My table, as *this* table here, never becomes a thought, to be sure, but *from* this table there comes to be in me a thought, or something thought.—Answer β) as to the peculiarity that I did not, after all, want to say here, as from No. 8 on, *nothing represented in a thinking comes of it*: it happened according to the *analysis* [p. 103] which may first, under the progressive development of what is at first comprehended in *One*, undertake the requisite separations, if it does not want, instead of *one* thread in thinking, to get ten at once, and thereby inevitably confuse itself. I therefore helped myself at first, where I had to put thinking in connection with matter, with the latter merely by prepositions.

As to the difficulty mentioned under No. 30, which with regard to the complete discussion of *time* still seems to remain, I hope that the ease with which it can be removed by the above explanation of it will contribute so much to the corroboration of the latter that it should henceforth be subject to no further doubt.

That time, *as time*, is a *thought*, we ourselves confess every time we bring it into the reckoning, which means just: every time we make *actual and present* use, in a thinking, of the infinitely repeated positing of A as A in it (let it be in itself what it will). The only question left, then, is: *of what* is it a thought? Answer: of a *succession*, or of preceding alterations, *not as something altered*, [p. 104] but *as* alterations, in short as a *succession*. Here I believe I may appeal simply to the consciousness of each one. Time is therefore a *succession* of the representations in a thinking, drawn into thinking. But as something *already* drawn by *us* into thinking, it can never be the condition of the possibility of any outer or inner apprehension; this would be a Kantian ὕστερον πρότερον [*hysteron proteron*]. If it cannot do this without contradiction: then it cannot do it *as time*; and if it cannot do it as *time*: then it can do it either not at all, or else only the *succession* in it (hence no longer time *as time*) can be the condition of the possibility of certain apprehensions. The latter is actually the case. For without a *succession* there can never come to be an application of a thinking (in man); let this thinking be applied now to *thinking* itself, or to an object. Always and in every case it presupposes, for the possibility of its application, an alteration proceeding from outside, an *act* (*actus*), an *impulse* through matter, what one may call, for all I care, a something of the *impulse* (through animal life), only not [p. 105] already a time, which, *as time*, first comes with the application of thinking. This succession is accordingly presupposed in every possible thinking in its *application*, and without it there would be, in man, *nothing thought at all of any kind*, hence neither inner nor outer apprehensions, hence no experiences of any kind. Was the difficulty, then, dangerous? It was none; for what was said above had only to be repeated and explained. Here again there befell *Kant* what always befalls him,—*a concrescence in his phantasy*, and this time indeed of A even with C. What was thought in time, *as time*, was fused in him with the, as he indeed assumes, *empirical* representation of an alteration; and, instead of saying: without a preceding alteration (without a succession) neither inner nor outer apprehensions would be possible, he says, without *time*, i.e. without A infinitely repeatable as A in the *succession* of the representations, i.e. without a thinking, already gone before in us, of the *succession*, in the application of a thinking to a succession, no succession of the representations would be possible. Then time might well grow long for one, before one came to a representation. [p. 106] And how did it fare, once more, with the poor animals?—These arrived at nothing at all of a representation; for *thinking* they notoriously cannot do.—But there is still something left over, which must not be allowed to lie unnoticed. If a succession is to be representable in a thinking *so that* it *must* be thought in the sense determined in No. 27: then it can never be annihilated in a thinking through thinking. If the succession, *as succession*, can never be annihilated in thinking through thinking: then also the condition of the possibility of this succession, the *impulse*, or the something of the *impulse*, or animal life, cannot be annihilated in thinking through thinking, but *must* rather be thought; as may already be evident from this, that otherwise thinking, in an application of itself, would not be awakened by this *impulse*, if thinking annihilated this impulse in a thinking. The impulse *must* accordingly be thought, as long as it remains impulse for a thinking, i.e. the *life* of man, *as life*, must be thought by him, as long as it remains in him impulse for a thinking. The life of man as *life* is his consciousness *that* (not how) he *lives*. The consciousness *that he lives* (consequently [p. 107] also that which is essentially connected with it, *as with an impulse*, *feeling, as feeling, drives as drives*), this consciousness *that he lives*, can therefore never be annihilated in man, *as man*, in thinking through thinking, but *must* rather, together with its essential appendage, be thought by him. It is a *form*, but precisely not a form of *thinking as thinking*, but of *material*; and indeed of material *insofar as an impulse goes out from it for thinking*. Hence the *simplicity* (not of the essence, but of the *act*), hence the *uncomprehendability* (not unthinkability or incomprehensibility) in all that comes to stand under the impulse *as impulse*; under the succession, as a *succession*, in the series of events in us, (such as feelings, drives, inclinations, *as this*)! Nothing betrays, *precisely because of the simplicity* of the *act* of an *impulse*, more approximation to the essence of thinking, notwithstanding that it is certainly still no thinking itself, than *feeling*. From it, as a *mere act*, *extension* falls away entirely, notwithstanding that without extension of that which first made the act possible, without extension of the material, there would presumably never have become an act, an [p. 108] impulse for a thinking.—Enough of the something of the *impulse*; for now the order comes also to a second, equally essential, question. It is: how does man also obtain a something of the *thing*, not merely of the impulse to a thing, (indeed of the impulse to the application of a thinking in general)? In other words: how does he now also obtain a *represented-in-thinking* (as that which we have so far called simply a something), *how* does he obtain an *object*?

Judging only by what has gone before, I am very much inclined to let even the animals enjoy my reflection a little from time to time too. Instead, therefore, of starting out in man from *consciousness*, as the supposedly highest point in humanity, as *Kant* did, I seek, with regard to *consciousness*, first only to win from animal life an advantage for my cognition. To that end I therefore take up animal life, which seems to have already done me noticeable service as mere impulse, once more, and take it up this time as a whole. What does it bring, besides the impulse, what does it bring as a whole, where it meets a thinking, for this thinking with it? It brings with it—

[p. 109] Not only *a* But also *a*

*Something of the impulse; Something of the thing,* Feeling, Apprehension Consciousness that one lives, Apperception Drives as drives, Representations

A desiring as desiring, *The Total* One *Individuality.*

Both series I therefore now comprehend first of all under the expression of an *individuality* in that sense which has been determined here. This individuality in the *sense just determined* must be, for a thinking, as thinking, material (=C), and in order to be able to take it up into a thinking, thinking must accordingly annihilate everything in this individuality that is material, *not form*, in it: for should this individuality ever be a Not-material for thinking, consequently not let itself, like every other material too, be worked over by thinking: then let me have it made comprehensible what it should or could otherwise and apart from this be for thinking, *as thinking*? Half-material perhaps, coagulated with a half-thinking, a concrescence of *Kantian* categories, as they, in a *Kantian* phantasy, [p. 110] lie there preformed? I am able to think nothing in that. Material, then, is *this* animal life itself together with its appurtenances, in short the whole individuality in the sense of the word *determined above* is material for a thinking *as thinking*. I have and find otherwise nothing but *thinking* and *material*, before the former has arranged something to itself out of the latter in an *object*. And if one has nothing otherwise either, without inventing: then let me at least keep it, until we all together once get something better.

If *this* animal life was previously exchanged for an individuality in general: then I now exchange the individuality again for a plus in general. Animal life as a *this* in *individual* becoming-conscious, individuality, I therefore now call simply that *plus* required for the possibility of an application of (human) thinking. In this *plus* that which in it is material must be annihilated in thinking through thinking; otherwise it would not thereby come to a thought. But there must also be something in it that cannot be annihilated in thinking through thinking; otherwise it would not thereby come to a something, as a something *represented* in thinking. If this *plus* is material: then everything in it can be annihilated that does not belong to the *outside-one-another* of the same in general, to the [p. 111] *succession and juxtaposition* in it in particular, and (as the test showed, as the condition of the possibility of a succession in representations) what does not belong to the *impulse*. This *plus* of an animal life will accordingly, as a thought, have nothing more in itself than the impulse indestructible in thinking through thinking, an *outside-, after-, beside-one-another*, which must first be filled out *empirically*, i.e. through individual feelings *as individual*, through individual representations *as individual*, or characteristically determined in its singularization. Here, my gentlemen, is your I, with all its mischief, which it still carries on behind the ruins of worlds and high above the thousand times thousand suns, according to the *Appellation* (pp. 113, 114). It is a concrescence in your phantasy, finer than those of your sect-head throughout are; it is the first stirring of a thinking announcing itself again, after its Critical guillotining, although under strong convulsions, in younger German heads. You have raised your heads up again; but you carried them, for that reason, so much the more degradingly for everyone else in the transcendental fog from which, once and for all, since the Königsberg epoch, no emerging was any longer possible at German universities. You again thought [p. 112] by halves, and half-thinking is not-thinking; hence the comical aspect of your philosophy for common sense, hence the bright soap-bubble of your I for the same.—How so?—Answer: Your I is nothing other than what every object, *as object*, also is; only that the *impulse* (of *animal life* in you), indestructible in thinking through thinking, also adheres to it indestructibly, on account of which indestructible appendage you then ascribe to yourselves *this* object (which, incidentally, is and remains like every other object) as your inalienable selfhood, and *must* ascribe. But if your I, (leaving aside now the adhering *impulse*), is an object: then it is an *outside-, after-, beside-one-another*, which, like all these *outside-, after-, beside-one-another*, would be quite meager in itself if it were not filled out *empirically* (material-containingly), and only thereby characteristically determined. *Empirically, however, your outside-, after-, beside-one-another is filled out in you* through your individual feelings as *individual*, through your individual representations, as *individual*; and herewith your I, *as I*, is finished: for you are characteristically determined in your singularization. I find myself in a position to give you on this point yet another, presumably unexpected, piece of information, which is offered me by animal [p. 113] life, converted into a *plus* (for the *application* of a thinking), and through which this very conversion of it into a *plus* might show itself, from yet another point of view, as other than that of a mere game. I started out from reckoning (§ 1); I spoke subsequently, merely in passing, of a subtracting, multiplying and dividing in the business of thinking. Many a one, I know for certain, has turned up his nose at this. This does not trouble me; I therefore continue and now *add* in thinking too, so that, when it is time for it, I may no longer speak *merely in passing* of a subtracting, multiplying and dividing in the business of thinking. One is posited, (A = 1). But once one remains one forever; and yet man comes out to *two*. But if he has once come out to this *two*: then it seems that it goes its assigned way to the end of his life with the *beside- and after-*, the *away-from- and toward-one-another*, the multiplying and dividing of what has already been multiplied. How then does he come out to *two*, notwithstanding that once one remains one forever? Surely unfailingly through a *plus*. To seek this *plus* in the “*once one is forever one*” would be a contradiction; *plus* is, as plus, not yet an *in and through*, notwithstanding that every *plus* [p. 114] must lead in the end to a plus, as an *in and through*. Be it, however, in itself what it will: it would still be a contradiction, in the “*once one is forever one*” *as a* once-one-is-forever-one, to seek yet besides a *plus*. For the possibility of the application of *that* one there must therefore come in addition a *plus*, hence a something. This something cannot be *that* one itself again; for otherwise it would have no *plus*, but only itself again. This something must, however, also be of such a constitution that *that* one can accept the same; how else would one bring *that* one and *this* something together? Were this something a *one*, and yet not *that* one: then they might fit together. Were this something no one, and *that* one could make it into a one: then they might fit together. Were this something e.g. *material*, and the material had a form, and this form were, *as form*, indestructible, and *that* one could annihilate everything in the material except its form: then *that* one could make this something into a one, and *that* one and this something made into one might fit together, would *have to* fit together, because no form can annihilate the other any more. We would therefore have 1 + 1, i.e. we would have come out to our [p. 115] Two, and hence to all our above reckoning-business in an *applied* thinking. At the latter One you have arrived, you gentlemen with the I, (hence the whole world had to sit in your I too,) and have supposed that you had thereby already broken through to the former One. But no; there was still something grown into your phantasy, as may be gathered without difficulty from the *Once-One*. I now notice well what will least please you about your dissected I, so openly do its *viscera* now lie there. It is without doubt the *beside-one-another* in it. Do not let that trouble you; it stays so. Above it was asserted that the outside-one-another already includes a beside-one-another, at least in *my* (applied) thinking. That this was no arbitrary assertion, but a thinking, I could now demonstrate by a mere appeal to the more developed nature of a *something*, or of a *plus*, required for the possibility of the application of a thinking. That something, or this *plus*, must namely be opposed to the constitution of *A as A in A through A*, otherwise nothing would come of it in what is *represented* in thinking. If it is to be opposed to the constitution of *A as A in A through A*, and one calls it B: then it cannot be B *as B*, [p. 116] but it must be B *outside* B, it cannot be B *in* B, but it must be B *after* B, it cannot be B *through* B, but it must be B *beside* B. Yet I will not let it rest here, but rather ask besides: how would it then be with the impulse, upon which that B (*cum annexis*) can first follow in an applied thinking, if that which is first to make the impulse were no *Outside-* and no *Beside-one-another*? I will ask once more how it would then even be possible to arrive at something as something *represented* in a thinking, be it this or that, an *I*, as you say, or a *Not-I*, i.e. object, provided with the animal impulse's appendage that is indestructible in thinking, or object without the same in representations which come from outside? Extension *must* therefore exist, in favor of the *impulse*, if it is to be able to bring with it a something for thinking; for what else would this something be? A not-something; what would the impulse be? A not-impulse. But that this extension should exhibit its essence precisely *in three spatial dimensions* in bodies, this we indeed learn, as finished-become, i.e. to *thinking* arrived men, only through a sight, connected with A, *hence already drawn into a thinking*, [p. 117] of these bodies themselves, and learn and cognize it *thereby*, because these three spatial dimensions are precisely that in those bodies which is indestructible through thinking in thinking, which bears to us an *apodictically* certain testimony of the extension, otherwise indeed eternally closed to our becoming-conscious without the preceding heed to a body-world lying before us, *as extension in three spatial dimensions*. — Now I can go further: thinking has its object, in the object of the geometer together with the reckoner he is, its science,—the I has, *as I*, its prophecy (*Weissung*).

### §. 14.

*Thinking, as thinking, suffers no difference of modality in the Kantian sense of the word; but is rather the unchangeable absolute condition of the possibility of any modality of merely empirical assertions; in itself, however, it remains always equally necessary and without alteration of its eternal laws, hence A infinitely many times repeatable as A in all possible cases of its use.*

[p. 118] Observation.

This is also clear from the fact that what is connected in a thinking, *as connected in a thinking*, can never be denied. Only if it is not connected in thinking, i.e. is a contradiction, can it be denied. In other words: that something is a *thought*, that hence the matter in it has been annihilated by its connection in a thinking, can never be denied, if it is a thought, no contradiction. The difference of modality, whether something may be asserted as yet merely *problematically* (*cum formidine oppositi*) [with fear of the opposite], or already *assertorically* (*absque formidine oppositi*) [without fear of the opposite], or even *without any rejoinder to be feared from any man* (*tanquam id, quod in confesso est*) [as that which is admitted], must accordingly rest on the *empirical*. Quite correctly, therefore, *Maaß*, l. c., p. 92, reckons the difference of modality merely to that “*which takes place just so only in this or that judging subject*,” and which hence belongs in no Logic given out as *Pure* and besides *General*. Why the *Kantians* (*Kiesewetter* in particular) in their *Pure* and besides *General* (a *Critical* surplus) Lo- [p. 119] gics proceed so entirely without scruple everywhere with the difference of modality, as something *Purely Logical*, will become clear from the total confusion which their sect-head caused especially in this article, in that he raised judgments only up to consciousness, and then took what was empirical to be *pure*.

Corollaries.

1) What is merely thought in a concept as in a representation, that is absolutely necessary and suffers no difference of modality.

2) The copula in *categorical* judgments, be they universal, particular or singular, be they affirming or denying, is absolutely necessary and without difference of modality; or, what in every possible categorical judgment is *merely* thought (hence *formally* and properly *logical* in the hitherto sense of the word) that is absolutely necessary and without difference of modality.

*Rem.* “The strict universal affirmative *assertoric* judgments, which admit no exception, are,” it says in *Kiesewetter*, l. c., p. 56, “*apodictic*.” Now the question arises: what then are these strict, universal affirmative, *assertoric* judgments, which [p. 120] here declared *apodictic*, supposed to be?—They are 1) *strict*; and strict they are, in *Kiesewetter*, not because they need no *Omne* [all] any longer: the possibility of a being-beyond all difference of quantity, hence the essence of *thinking* as well as of the *object*, hence *Logic*, never came to his mind in his whole Logic; and he indeed himself straightway opposes to them the *particularly* affirmative judgments. It is accordingly not the strict universality which, needing no *Omne* any longer, already attaches to these judgments as to something *thought*, and in which the *particular-asserting* (e.g. a particular triangle) would be a contradiction; but they are through a prefixed *Omne*, consequently according to human world-sections in experience, consequently *empirically* strict, i.e. not strict. They are 2) *universally affirmative*; is there then in affirming, *as affirming*, a particularity, or is there not here a synkrasis of the *act* of affirmation partly with the number of those affirming, partly with that of what is affirmed? The unlogical expressions, still descending from the old logical formula: *Asserit A. e. c.* [A asserts, etc.], of a *Universally-* or *Particularly-affirming*, *Universally-* or *Particularly-denying* [p. 121] proposition, which, in a perverse way, draw quantity (according to language) to affirming or denying, one should, to avoid such misunderstandings, rather give up entirely, and put in their place: *Affirmed Universal*, *Denied Universal* Judgments, etc., to which in *Kantian* Logics there would still have to be put: universal-affirmed, universal judgments and universal-denied, universal judgments; otherwise they would not be *Kantian*-apodictic. *Kiesewetter* meant to say: they are something universally *affirmed*, partly with regard to the testimony of all men, partly with regard to the whole complexion of apprehensions which they express. They are 3) *assertoric*, i.e. according to *Kant* (hence also according to *Kiesewetter*) their affirming is merely actual, in *Kiesewetter’s* language (p. 41): The union of their marks in a unity of consciousness has merely already taken place, and yet they are supposed also to be *apodictic*, i.e. because their affirming is merely *actual*, so it is supposed to be also *necessary*.—Here he was now *Kantianly* entangled in contradictions, which, behind the synkrasis No. 2 and behind the epithet of the *Strict*, empty *in this connection*, quite properly hidden from him [p. 122] kept. In plain *German* and unconfused, his proposition namely runs: if a judgment is once universally affirmed, partly with regard to the testimony of all men, partly with regard to the whole complexion of apprehensions which it expresses: then it is apodictic. An assertion without exception, as well with regard to the number of men asserting as also with regard to what is asserted, in short *an experience without exception*, is therefore to him something *apodictic*. Since now an assertion without exception takes place in a *singular* judgment as in a *universal* one: so with him a *singular* judgment too (which he surely would not have conceded) is a *strictly universal affirmative* assertoric judgment, and is only then again, as strictly universal affirmative and *assertoric*, also at the same time *apodictic*. Since he further (if it is no misprint, cf. p. 46, § 102) on the same page counts the *singular* judgments among the *particular* ones: so with him a *particular* judgment too is a *strictly universal affirmative, assertoric judgment without exception*, hence *apodictic*, and yet he himself says l. c.: “the *particularly* affirmative judgments can be problematic, [p. 123] assertoric and apodictic.” So great is the confusion on this single page in *Kiesewetter*, and this confusion is genuinely *Kantian*.—*Kant* had namely, in order to serve his table of categories as is due, augmented the quantity, quality and relation of a judgment, already recognized before, with something new, under the title *Modality*, i.e. with the manner of the reception of a judgment in consciousness, or, in his language, with the gradual incorporation of a judgment into the understanding? (p. 101, l. c.). On this occasion there befell him the misfortune that he fell down from transcendental philosophy into the trivial empirical psychology, a misfortune which one could have prophesied to him, because he did not know what *thinking* is, and therefore, in critiquing the *Pure* Reason, never looked beyond consciousness, hence beyond the animal in the human child. This latter is confirmed by the *present* case itself already, in that it was possible for the Father of Critique to let something be incorporated *gradually* into the understanding. Such a *gradual* understanding in the business of incorporating into it is no thinking, hence no understanding *as a thinking*, hence [p. 124] merely nothing else than light or shade in a certain animal becoming-conscious, behind which then first lies that thinking, unchangeable *as thinking*, modality-less, in *our* class of beings. As soon as the old Logical footing of the quantity, quality and relation of a judgment gave out on him, and he now wanted to go further out transcendentally, it went, instead of upwards, downwards. It is, by everyone’s reckoning, an experience that, when one sees a man in the distance and does not yet see rightly, one is wont to say it is *possible* that this be a man (*Problematic* judgment; deposits: a category of *possibility*, consisting in the *approach*, or a *gradual* light for the animal becoming-conscious). It is, by everyone’s reckoning, a second experience, that when the man comes nearer and one sees him better, one is wont to say that is *actually* a man, (*Assertoric* judgment; deposits a category of *existence*, consisting in the *presence*, or a *decisive* light for the animal becoming-conscious). It is, by everyone’s reckoning, a third experience, that, when the man is now once there, and the whole society [p. 125] sees him, but one of the society still wanted to speak against it, and say it was no man, one would lead him to the man, and *show* the unbeliever (*monstrate* through eye, ear and touch) that this *must* be a man; or, if the man has already passed by, and one no longer saw him, but one of the society still wanted to speak against it, and say the man was still there, one would convince him by the not-seeing-any-more, not-hearing-any-more, not-being-able-to-touch-any-more of the man, that the existence of the man was now given to him *only still through its possibility*, but no longer through his senses, hence he *must* now, so *Kant* concluded, p. 111, the non-existence of this man, by virtue of the givenness of the existence of the same *through a mere possibility*, *unavoidably* admit. (*Kantian-apodictic* judgment; deposits a category of *necessity*, consisting partly in a *presence confirmed* through eye, ear and touch, and a decisive light *still supported* through ear and touch, for the animal becoming-conscious; partly in a not-seeing-any-more, not-hearing-any-more, not-being-able-to-touch-any-more, [p. 126] hence in an *indisputably* certain *shadow* for the animal becoming-conscious). That this presentation of the *Kantian* categories of modality is the only possible one I can prove generally from the fact that he had indeed already dispatched the, hitherto so-called, *Logical* reality (affirmation *considered in itself*) under the class of *categories of quality* (in company with negation and limitation as the *thought-forms* assigned to it?), hence nothing more at all could remain to him, for the category of *existence*, than the *local* existence, i.e. approach, or presence, removal or local vanishing. That, however, in particular the *Kantian* necessity is here drawn according to its nature becomes evident because 1) according to p. 110 “the third category everywhere arises from the connection of the second with the first of its class,” hence in the present case necessity (as the third of its class) must arise from the connection of the category of existence (as the second) with the category of possibility (as the first); existence, still connected with possibility, however, being nothing else than local presence, forced upon one, (in *Kant apodictic or necessary* [p. 127] made) through the *senses*, (this would be the *light* in the animal becoming-conscious, the first half of the category of *necessity*); because 2) according to p. 111 “necessity is nothing else than the existence which is given through possibility itself,” the givenness of an existence (of a *being-here*) through the *mere possibility*, however, can be nothing else than a not-seeing-any-more, not-hearing-any-more, not-being-able-to-touch-any-more of that which one formerly saw, heard or could touch, and because, finally, a necessity, as an *existence given through the possibility itself*, can be nothing else than an *extorted*, *unavoidable* and *Kantian-apodictic* confession (*in confesso est*) [it is admitted], obtained by a not-seeing-any-more, not-hearing-any-more, not-being-able-to-touch-any-more, that this existence, now given through a mere possibility, is a not-any-more-existence, an absence, a *shadow* for an animal becoming-conscious (= 0).—Herewith we mortals too would be measured out from this side in the *Critique*. Beyond the *digito monstrare, aut digito non amplius monstrare posse* [to point with the finger, or to be no longer able to point with the finger], their yardstick in this regard does not go. Of *Demonstrating* no talk; [p. 128] that would be *Wolffian*: of the necessary of *thinking as a thinking*, of the *object as object*, no talk; that would be dialectical: it skims over the pointing of the finger, and where something skims over the pointing of the finger, there arises the necessity of a givenness of its existence *through the mere possibility*, hence there arises through this mere possibility, in which its existence is now given, a *Kantian-necessary* and *Kantian-apodictic* givenness of its not-any-more-existence for an animal becoming-conscious, in so far as to an animal becoming-conscious an existence (as being-here, being-there) can be given only through seeing, hearing, touching. Of possibility and actuality, as the essential character of every possible object in a thinking in general, not merely as equivalent (according to the language of life) with a presence in the representation of an animal consciousness either not yet bestowed or already bestowed, no talk: this would already point to a being, and a being belongs, as is well known, first in the *practical* department, after it has known how, *theoretically*, through nothing but *mere thinking* (through thought-forms, paired with syllogisms), *Ideas* to [p. 129] prepare. But should now really, from *Problematic* judgments, a category of *possibility*, from *Assertoric* ones a category of *existence*, from *Apodictic* ones a category of *necessity*, be able to be lifted out, as the thought-form lying each time at the ground of such a judgment; should *existence* and *possibility*, connected with each other, according to p. 110, give the thought-form of *necessity*, or, according to p. 111, *necessity* be nothing else than a *givenness of existence through possibility*: then it would follow that according to p. 110 an Assertoric judgment, with which something Problematic has been combined, would *therefore* have to become Apodictic, because something Problematic and Assertoric came together in it, or, according to p. 111, *therefore*, because the Assertoric of a judgment (existence) would already be given through the Problematic in it (through possibility itself). Thus stand the matters; not even to the *unlocal*, i.e. provided with no *express* indication of place, presence of a representation in consciousness, not even to *that* manner of the gradual incorporation of a representation into the same (as *Kant* speaks), [p. 130] which the man can bring about by a subsequent development of that which was represented to him therein, either himself or with the help of others, not even to light and shadow, distinctness or confusion in the becoming-conscious of *concepts*, was any regard thus had in the *Kantian* division of judgments into *problematic*, *assertoric* and *apodictic*; but *Kant’s* whole modality is properly *palpability*, which, where it does not become wholly tangible, produces merely the category of possibility; where it becomes tangible, the category of existence; where it becomes tangible out of the not wholly tangible, according to p. 110, or else, where it, according to p. 111, drops away, and leaves behind only the dregs of the possibility *of an existence* in consciousness, the category of *necessity*.
