And such a philosophy could insist on *principles*? A philosophy that traces the *necessary* and *apodictic* back to intuitions, pure or coarse, and the pure again to the coarse (the five fingers, l. c.), a philosophy that attaches to thinking, there where it runs out beyond palpability, the scorn of an empty Dialectical illusion, could only speak a reasonable [p. 131] word about principles? Beardless striplings, who, crawled behind the aegis of your master, mocked Germany’s *Leibniz* and *Mendelssohn*, because they had not yet, as you fancied, walked the path of principles, what were principles in your mouth? Nonsense such as the gloomiest Scholastic never let come from him. But of you there can be no question at all; bluster as you will, in societies and in public papers. You are and remain to me ridiculous people. Your forever warped head may *Kant* answer for, to whom I return. He was so clever as to adopt the tone of principles in *philosophy* only far back in the Critique, where the slain theoretical reason was shortly to rise again *as practical*, and then finally came, in his *Critique of Practical Reason*, on the occasion of the imperative in a moral law, which he calls a fact, and indeed the *only* fact of pure reason (hence the *only* fact of *thinking*? pp. 56, 72, 73), to that with which he should have begun the Critique of *Theoretical* Reason,—to *thinking*, consequently only there to a *more than palpable* existence (a being), consequently [p. 132] only there to principles, i.e. to a *prius* as necessary condition of the possibility of such an imperative, as he meant, and which he believed he had found straightway in *freedom*. To freedom, therefore, the strongest minds among his adherents also tied their principles (words) one after another; and because they did not know theoretically how they stood with thinking, but thinking had in them theoretically, after genuinely *Kantian manner*, grown together with C, hence with the material, and they consequently *there* saw beyond animal consciousness as little as *Kant*: so it went with them as was described above, they posited the chimera of a (*Kantian*) imperative of freedom, as the sole supersensible lever for them, (as sole fact of the *pure*, hence also sole fact of *their* reason) into their I, concresced with the world, and produced, with the help of the same, now through their I the whole world, which, as a C already concresced with thinking in them anyway, hence as *their* caricature, was quite easy to push off by means of that lever; (*Critical*, upon an *I fertilized with freedom* built, *Idealism*, now also fully provided with its *prius*).

[p. 133] —What, however, is now to be done at all with that late offspring of a *Kantian*-practical reason, begetting principles only as *practical*? Nothing more.—*Conclamatum est* [It is all over]. For either it is a thinking, and then it is dialectical; or it is no thinking, and then it is no reason. These are the pure principles of the wretched brood of *Kantian* philosophasters, (as *Nicolai* so aptly calls them).—Freedom, understood in the *Kantian* way, as necessary condition of the possibility of an imperative, is a thinking as necessary condition of the possibility of a thinking (considered still apart from its application); hence a thinking (considered still apart from its application), as *prius*, and again a thinking (considered still apart from its application) as *posterius*, hence, understood in the *Kantian* way, again nothing; but a freedom such as is already given in the imperative of the Once-One, a freedom with which a man, from whom thinking, *as a thinking*, remained hidden, merely described, without knowing it, thinking itself *one-sidedly*. Because, however, he did not know this: so, e.g., all postulates of his doctrine of right are postulates without their head; *reason wills just so*; further he never could go; for theoretically [p. 134] thinking had entered into its rest, and Germany had only principles *of willing* left, headless principles therefore: *volo, jubeo, mando* [I will, I command, I order]. To this comes the further inconvenience that precisely in the practical and in acting, the total impossibility of making use of one’s thinking, hence of one’s freedom, without an *each time* and indeed *noticeably* preceding animal impulse, is most striking of all; hence the toils under which *Kant*, in his *Critique of Practical Reason*, with his freedom, (as an at least *purely thinkable* premise for an acting), had to seek to evade *time* (i.e. all possible circumstances). In the series downward and into life, namely, −B can absolutely not take place without a B standing before it. Now, however, the impulse, as a something indestructible in thinking through thinking, can in its whole detail quite fittingly be placed under our B; the *Kantian* imperative, however, with its detail, under −B as the *merely thought* in the something, and what has been said accordingly be made graspable through the following scales:

[p. 135] B − B

Impulse of animal life Imperative

Drives Ought

Desiring Willing

Understanding (as A posited in B) Reason (as A posited in − B)

Grounds of motivation Willing for the sake of the ought

Prudence (as mature deliberation, *prudentia*) Duty

Good conduct (καθῆκον) Virtue (κατόρθωμα)

Opportunity from without (τύχαι) Moral merit

Health of the powers of mind Well-being (as − B, as good conscience).

*Logical* is this opposition, under the alteration here introduced, without blame; for nothing of what occurs in the one series is already included in the mere thinking of that which occurs in the other: A, as A, although unchangeable, *as this*, is nevertheless, as A posited in B, in [p. 136] and with this B alone, not also at the same time to be considered as posited in (− B); otherwise one would consider B and (− B) as distinguished and yet also as not distinguished, i.e. one would fall into a contradiction. But *practically* it cannot subsist as an opposition, because no (− B) downwards in the row and into life occurs without a preceding B; consequently what stands under B must in all cases precede, before it can come to that which stands under − B, which already strikes common sense most noticeably *precisely in the practical*, notwithstanding that in every representation and every concept the same case must throughout occur. A *homo Noumenon* in a *homo Phænomenon* is therefore a nonentity; for as *Phænomenon* he is an individual, as *Noumenon* no individual, hence in this respect also no longer a *homo*. — Further, and once more back to modality itself, in order then to be able to determine what necessity is, not *Kantianly*, but actually. First to possibility! — It is supposed, according to *Kant*, to be that which, in the analysis of a problematic judgment, is found as its primal form, which hence makes every problematic judgment a *problematic* [p. 137] judgment. Does possibility, then, belong only to problematic judgments, or is it not the essential character of what is thought in general, *as a thing thought*? Can it, consequently, ever be made exclusively the distinguishing character of that which a man does *not yet know for certain*? Is it not a superficiality unpardonable in a philosopher if, from the problematic or uncertain of a sense-impression, which the language of life may designate by a mere possibility (of being-able-to-be-given in a representation), he believes he has found the essence of possibility itself, as of a thought, and has thereby, under the name of a form of thought for everything problematic, entirely and utterly dispatched it? — If one analyzes an *assertoric* judgment, there is supposed to be found, as its primal form, a certain category of *existence*. If in the language of life that is called *possible* which one indeed cannot reject as a *so-called thought*, but which yet is not *present* in one’s consciousness by means of any determinate representation as that for which it is given out: then in that very same language one calls that [p. 138] *actually existing, asserts or maintains* it, which one not only cannot reject as a *so-called* thought, but also has *present* in one’s consciousness in a determinate representation, be it one’s own apprehension or testimony, be it true or false. And in *this* actuality of local presence in representations, which lies solely in a, *Kantianly* interpreted, asserting, a, *Kantianly* interpreted, maintaining, that it is one’s own apprehension or testimony, that it is true or false, as its *sufficient ground*, can a philosopher let it rest *there*, *where he*, by virtue of the thought of possibility—placed here in *one* class with existence—would have had to give information about the *thought* of an actuality in general? Hence *Kiesewetter* above needed so many epithets in order to screw an assertoric judgment up to an apodictic one; hence it had first to become a *universally* affirmative one, i.e., if it is to have a sense, a *universally-affirmed* judgment, before it could pass over into a *Kantian*-apodictic one. If, finally, one analyzes an *apodictic* judgment, there is supposed to be found, as its primal form, a certain category of *necessity*. [p. 139] This necessity is now represented partly as an existence given through possibility itself (p. 111), partly as something sprung from the connection of existence with possibility (p. 110). If one now does not divide it (as happened above, in order to become master of the confusion) into two necessities, of which the one has local vanishing, the other local existence as its basis, but takes it, as is fitting, only as one and the selfsame in thinking: then *Kant*’s idea of a necessity, considered as *one* and the selfsame idea, destroys itself of its own accord. For an existence which is already given through possibility can certainly be no existence that should first spring from *a connection of existence* with possibility; (if something is already given in another itself, how can it then first spring from a connection of it with that other still to be undertaken?) But now, according to p. 111, necessity is nothing other than the existence which is given through possibility itself; consequently it cannot, without contradiction, any longer, as occurs on p. 110, be represented as something sprung from the connection of existence with possibility. [p. 140] We wish, however, once for all to assume 1) necessity to be nothing other than an existence given through possibility itself: then this existence is either a (*local*) existence, or an actuality (as *thought*). If it is, as *Kant* assumes, a (*local*) existence: then necessity is thus an existence, (*local*), given through possibility itself. But now an existence (*local*) given through possibility itself is a local non-existence (as regards the *present*); consequently necessity, as an existence (*local*) given through possibility itself, would be a local non-existence (as regards the *present*), a shadow = 0, since with it, it is indisputably certain, actual intuition is over and done; hence *Kiesewetter*, quite in the spirit of this *one half* of a *Kantian* necessity (the *prius* thereto he did not know), l. c. p. 56, reminds us: “the negative judgments cannot be problematic, and the *universally negative* (i.e. the *universally-negated*, general) *assertoric* judgments are *apodictic*”, (like the shadow = 0, of which nothing more can be said with *indisputable certainty* than of [p. 141] an intuited actual object). —

If, however, what *Kant* does not assume (in that he had already previously dispatched reality as a *form of thought*), this existence given through possibility itself is actuality (as *thought*); then necessity consists thus in an actuality given through possibility itself, in a *being*, which, as is well known, may first show itself in the *Critique of Practical Reason*, hence found nowhere any access in the table of categories or, as it would have to be called in German, table of thinking, as *being*, i.e. as a *thinking*. — We wish 2) to suppose (since this is indeed supposed to be the case with all classes of the categories according to p. 110, and so has the majority on its side anyway) that necessity, as the third category of its class, has sprung from the connection of the second of its class with the first, hence from the connection of existence with possibility: then this necessity has now either sprung from possibility *as something problematic*, and from existence *as something assertoric*, or it has not sprung from this. — If it has sprung from possibility as something *problematic*, and [p. 142] from existence *as something assertoric* (this *Kant* assumes); then it is a something which one could not reject even as a thought, and to which there was added besides the *presence* in certain determinate representations. Accordingly, however, it would be nothing further than again an *asserting*, resting on the *presence* of certain determinate representations, which could be either one’s own apprehensions or testimony, either true or false, and where, in order to become *Kantian-apodictic*, it would have to run out into the *Kiesewetterian* general affirming (into the *in confesso est* [it is conceded]). But if this necessity has not sprung from possibility *as something problematic*, and not from existence *as something assertoric*: then it can also have its ground neither in something problematic nor in something assertoric. If this necessity has its ground neither in something problematic nor in something assertoric: then it rests either not at all on possibility and existence, or it rests on possibility *as something non-problematic*, and on existence *as something non-assertoric*. Something that did not rest at all on possibility [p. 143] and yet was supposed to belong as a something (as object) in the system of world-beings, would be a contradiction; this necessity must accordingly rest on possibility (− B). Something that did not rest at all on existence would rest either on nothing and yet be supposed to rest on something, would hence again be a contradiction, or it would rest on existence (merely as nothing monstrable any more), hence on existence *as actuality* (B). If it is accordingly contradictory that this necessity should rest not at all on possibility, and not at all on existence (as *actuality* = B): then it must rest merely on possibility, *as something non-problematic*, merely on existence, *as something non-assertoric*. Possibility, *as nothing problematic any more*, but as absolutely *apodictic*, is what is thought itself, *as thought* (− B); existence, *as nothing assertoric any more*, but as absolutely *apodictic*, is what is thought *as a something thought*, i.e. as a something represented in thinking (B); consequently necessity rests 1) on what is thought itself, *as what is thought* 2) on what is thought, *as a something thought*, [p. 144] in a word on thinking, *as thinking* (A), on the object, *as object* (B − B). Necessity is merely the expression, now once put into general circulation, for what is *ineradicably* found in thinking *at thinking*, and in thinking *at what is represented in one thinking*, and *this* necessity (otherwise none, no mere *in confesso est* [it is conceded]) is *apodictic*; for it is given in possibility, as *nothing problematic any more*, but as something apodictic, in existence, as *nothing assertoric any more*, but as something apodictic (in *being*). — By this manner of developing the Critical chaos I hope to have made noticeable at the same time the obliqueness with which *Kant* has coupled existence with possibility. Possibility is pure thought; existence (with the possible opposition of a being-there, hence of a no-more-existence) is sense-work, appended in us, if I may so say, to the unalterable thought of an object, as soon as once C has been annihilated in thinking through A down to a B − B. If now an object comes before our senses: then the sense-impression which it makes on us finds the thought of the object, [p. 145] hence of possibility and actuality and of the necessity, enclosed in their thinking, already in place, and also joins itself, because it cannot be otherwise, equally to all three. Only then is it first left to *empirical* conditions whether we must also bestow upon this sense-impression, which, as object, is possible as actual, and actual as necessary, the (sensible) representation of a *local* actuality (of an existence e.g. as a human existence), or else the non-representation of a *local* actuality (of an existence e.g. as no human existence). In the former case we call, in the language of life, the representation, which in itself is always necessarily-possible, *now as representation*, necessarily actual, hence, *as non-representation*, merely possible; in the latter case, on the contrary, we call the non-representation, in itself always necessarily-possible, *now as non-representation*, necessarily actual, hence, as we are accustomed to say, *as representation*, merely possible. On the simple business of thinking, just described, as of an A posited in B − B, and of representation, as of a light (with its gradations,) or of a shadow (with its gradations) for animal becoming-conscious, rests the whole essence of demonstrating [p. 146] on the one side, of pointing a finger (monstrating) on the other. What is *still material-containing* sense-work cannot be demonstrated; what is a matter of thinking, *as a matter of thinking*, cannot be monstrated. That one could not demonstrate the existence of things outside us in philosophy, and where one was wise never wished to demonstrate it, before *Kant* also made this great discovery (Preface to the *Critique of Pure Reason*, p. XXXIX), came about because it is something monstrable, but just for that reason nothing demonstrable. *Kant* is by no means of this opinion. To him it remains always “a scandal of philosophy and of universal human reason, to have to take the existence of things outside us, from which we after all have the whole material for cognitions even for our inner sense, merely on *faith*, and, if it occurs to anyone to doubt it, to be unable to set against him any *satisfactory proof*.” Here the case, perhaps not rare, occurs that a *scandalum acceptum* [scandal taken] is the worst *scandalum datum* [scandal given], at least on the part of a master in philosophy. Yet this was consistent for the first, partly crude, partly pure, *philosophy of intuition* in the world, for a philosophy which, where it speaks of pure [p. 147] intuitions, remarks of God himself: “for *such* must all his cognition be, and not *thinking*, which always proves limits”, l. c. p. 71. It was consistent, once what is only demonstrable (God’s being) had been put in question by a philosopher as *monstrable*, conversely also to put in question what is only monstrable (the existence of a world of sense) as de*monstrable*. It could not well be expected otherwise, after the other specimens of a *Kantian* method of demonstration, as it went e.g. in his proof for the existence of an intelligible world, which we are to enjoy only *after* this life. May I be permitted to go into this proof a little, before more is added on demonstration. His major premise sounds *Stoic*: it runs:

Good conduct and well-being must be together; in other words, in the thinking of a duty fulfilled there is already also included the thinking of a state of mind, corresponding to this fulfillment of duty that has occurred, as − B, (in which the *Stoa* then sought the sole purely human perfection or blessedness, the *finis bonorum*),

[p. 148] Under this *Stoic* pure proposition of reason, as its *major*, *Kant* now sets the exact counterplay in a *minor* 1) *limiting* the assertion of the major premise, 2) empirical, thus destroys his major premise again as a pure, hence indestructible, proposition of reason; (for the limiting of a proposition of reason, instead of subjecting to it, as to an eternal rule, every *minor* in empiricism absolutely, is a lifting-off of its *universal* truth, hence a destruction of itself); in that he concludes:

But now this is not the case in the present life (*Critique of Practical Reason*, pp. 207, 208); consequently there must yet be another world.

Something like this might not have happened to a *Chrysippus*. No, the Stoic showed himself in his morals too as a logician. The proposition of reason remained for him what it is, an indestructible proposition of reason, whose truth the philosopher, as engaged in the judging of the world, had to hold fast against every suffering of the time (through circumstances), and, as an active sage, to realize through his whole manner of acting, in that he assumed the fulfillment of duty absolutely and without any other regard as the standard of the greatest possible perfection for man, *as man*, unremittingly [p. 149] and in this regard should never separate the *utile* [the useful] from the *honestum* [the honorable]. Cicero reminds us (*Off.* III, 3): *Socratem execrari solitum eos, qui primum haec (honestum et utile) natura cohaerentia, opinione distraxissent* [that Socrates used to execrate those who first severed in opinion these two (the honorable and the useful), which are coherent by nature]. So did the *sophists*, and so did *Kant*. On both sides it went by way of fallacies. — But to return to the above: the question arises: can the existence of things outside us really not be demonstrated? *Postulated*, so that we can think something, but by no means *demonstrated*; for 1) what can only be postulated is for that very reason indemonstrable, 2) demonstrating consists precisely in this, that one must annihilate for someone his intuitions about a given object of sense, as *mere intuitions of a certain material*, in order to bring it back, notwithstanding that it remains an object of sense for itself, as purified of the material, only in its *form*, hence solely in the *necessary* components which cling to it as object of sense *ineradicably*, to A in B − B; *pointing*, on the other hand, consists in this, that to the object B − B one in turn ascribes a determinate C in intuitions (or apprehensions):

(A + C) posited in (B − B + C) [p. 150] would thus be *pointing*, (monstrating, but not *dismissing* from the monstrable, i.e. not de-monstrating). But now nothing that is matter, *as matter*, can be taken up into a thinking: consequently *this* C too, *as matter*, must be annihilated in thinking, i.e. become a *thought*. If, however, *this* C is yet, *as* thought, to be distinguishable from B − B: then, besides what already lies in B − B, something must still adhere to it *as this C*, which makes it precisely a thought *of this C*. One comprehends that what thus adheres, in order to make it into *this* C in a thought, cannot lie in thinking itself, *as a thinking*, and, if it does not lie in thinking, yet *as matter* can just as little pass over into a thinking, as any C *as C*. What thus adheres must therefore pertain to C *as this C*, so much is clear; but it cannot pertain to it considered as mere material, cannot lie in it itself, considered as *mere material*, so much too is clear. If it must pertain to C *as this C*, and yet cannot pertain to it, considered as *mere material*: then it must pertain to it insofar as the material has been purified down to its form. This happens in the object, wherein the material [p. 151] has been annihilated down to the form of its juxtaposition, or down to extension, and down to the form of its succession, or down to alteration in itself and without further alterability. But this very thing we already have indeed in B − B, and what we are *now* seeking is a *plus* to B − B. A *plus* to B − B, which is to be neither a thinking, *as thinking*, nor a material, *as material*, seems, under the presupposition that in B − B extension too together with alteration without further alterability is already given, to be a perverse thing, which promises one, as to its existence, to point to Utopias. We simply cannot take any material into a thinking, i.e., as has already been often reminded, into the necessary representation of the object, as object in general, which is henceforth ineradicable for a thinking; this our investigation has stated so far. We may therefore not do so now either, for the sake of this *plus*; recall the syllogisms just built on that investigation. What counsel, then, with this *plus*? — It must turn up, if we recall to ourselves the manner and way in which it came about with the object above; for it is indeed once again a matter of an object, only with the difference that it is now to become a *this object*. Without a [p. 152] succession independent of it, our thinking would, as we saw, never arrive at an object, and without the inner life-impulse, ineradicable by thinking, *as thinking*, never arrive at a succession. But with this inner life-impulse is given merely the making-noticeable, ineradicable by thinking, of a certain determinate privation (feelings, drives, etc.); consequently merely a determinate *Minus* (subtraction) is laid down for the reception of a *Plus* (of an addition) in our animal becoming-conscious. In other words this means: the inner impulse, provided with the object at which it aims already by means of a *Minus*, i.e. *through a privation of it*, demands still something, adequate to it, active *externally*, equally ineradicable in thinking as it itself is. Besides the alteration from within, essentially required for the possibility of the application of a thinking and not to be altered by it, we should thus now also have found, as unavoidable, a determinate alteration from without, essentially required for the possibility of the application of a thinking and not to be altered by it; both of which can then very fittingly attach themselves to the likewise inalterable object (in B − B). Only the question is what this *external* alteration [p. 153] is to be, and in what it is to consist? In a determinate apprehension or sensible representation, as *this* representation, with this determinate, and *as for this* now inalterable juxtaposition, and *as for this* now inalterable succession. This apprehension, as act of a determinate sensible representation in our animal becoming-conscious, can with its essential determinations be as little eradicated by thinking, *as thinking*, as the life-impulse with its essential determinations; for otherwise one form would annihilate the other (an act, subsisting for itself, of an ineradicable *modus* would become an act, annihilated by another, for itself subsisting act of an ineradicable *modus*, hence an act of eradication on something ineradicable). If it cannot be annihilated as act: then there is thus in C, *as this C*, something that must be taken up into thinking, namely the act of its representation, as *this* representation; and the inalterable in a certain C, whereby it becomes suitable as *plus* for a B − B already ineradicable in itself, is hence the act of its representation in an animal becoming-conscious, as *this* representation, determined by a juxtaposition and succession given in it. If this act *must* [p. 154] be taken up into thinking: then the way is found out on which, notwithstanding the unavoidable eradication of the material in a certain C, in order to take it up as *this* C into a thinking, there can then, after its passage into a thinking has taken place, again attach itself everything that clung to it in the organs of conception of our five senses, and in our animal consciousness (not in thinking) may remain hanging, empirically-persistently, under manifold change, also for the future. This latter, namely, is merely a multiplicity in the manner of the existence of a material, given to us through our five senses as organs of conception, such as the seven primary colors, the seven primary tones, the simple impressions of feeling, smell and taste, which are anything but concepts (*perceptiones* or indeed *notiones tantummodo claræ* [perceptions, or indeed merely clear notions]) but rather a positing of our thinking (through B − B) into the original elements, ineradicable by our animal becoming-conscious, (sensible beginnings) of the multiplicity, under which the manner of existence of a material is given to us alternately through our five senses, and which are therefore forced upon our animal consciousness, as belonging to the existence of a material for us, by impulses from without, [p. 155] without our animal consciousness being able to eradicate them. But since they, the act of the impulse excepted, are yet eradicable in thinking in general, and yet no form can eradicate another; I must conclude that they are actually that *alterable something* in the material for me, in short that they are changing *affections* (*modi* in *existence* for my becoming-conscious, not ineradicable *modi*, as I have hitherto taken them, *not modi of being*), hence merely changing affections of the material *as material*, which in my (empirically-) persistently deposited image of certain objects of sense may have to be decorated now thus, now otherwise; hence they then also come to need a pointing of the finger or touching (in particular when the bloom of life is past) repeated from time to time, in order not to pass over too suddenly from the light, which they are for an animal consciousness, into a shadow for the same. *This* sense-work would thus be the *plus* of pointing at something extended, in order to fix it for conception in an animal becoming-conscious, would be the foundation of what *Kant* calls synthetic judgments *a posteriori*, the bare material in C, which hereby appears [p. 156] duly separated from our *plus*; in that our above formula for pointing,

(A + C) posited in (B − B + C)

has now resolved itself into the *formulas*,

I) (A + C) posited in (B − B + a determinate something, inalterable for that which lies before the senses, of an alteration and extension without further possible alteration in that which just now lies before the senses, *insofar as it just now lies before the senses*); to which then attaches itself again,

II) (A + C) posited in (B − B + an alterable something).

To point at the latter is called *pointing* or *Kantian* demonstrating; the former formula, however, grounds every kind of actual demonstration. We must therefore still dwell on this. Meanwhile I will for now merely remind in passing, 1) that the *plus* of a determinate something, inalterable for that which lies before the senses, of an alteration and extension without further possible alteration, in the former formula, gives the representation of a *substance* (*ejus, quod rebus substat* [of that which underlies things]), which by the *logician*, brought over from metaphysics, and referred merely to the thought-work on it under B, is called *subject* [p. 157] called, and which, under the − B, takes on a predicate; (but if − B, e.g. in virtue, is made subject, then this − B is related under B to a substance, e.g. virtue was the character of Socrates); 2) that in every possible *empirical* judgment (hence also concept), let it have for a quantity, quality, relation and modality whatever it will, the first formula must be expressed, and that it exhausts therein what is properly *formal*, hence *logical*; 3) that every possible *empirical* judgment, insofar as the first formula is expressed therein, is *analytic*, insofar as the second is expressed therein, is *synthetic*, consequently the beginning of the *Kantian* critique too in this respect consists in the beginning of an unpardonable superficiality in Germany’s newest philosophy. But as for the grounding of the whole business of demonstration through the formula of No. I, this will be clear from the following analysis. The object is (as A in − B) thinking itself without possibility of an extension or alteration. The object is (as A in B) extension itself without further possible alteration of the extended. The object is (likewise as A in B) alteration itself without further possible alteration of the alterable. Thinking lets itself, in extension and alteration, *as something* [p. 158] *further unalterable*, be posited absolutely. Extension lets itself be posited in alteration, so long as the latter is still something alterable (material-containing), *as determining it*, until the alteration becomes unalterable (in the object or in *this* object as thought), and extension itself proves to be an alteration of the extended no longer to be altered. Alteration lets itself be posited in extension, so long as the latter is still something extended (material-containing), *as determining it*, until the extended (as *material-containing*) is annihilated in thinking, and the alteration of the alterable, no longer to be altered, proves itself to be an extension. Thinking can accordingly be applied to an extension, *as extension*, without altering itself or it, to an alteration, *as alteration*, without altering itself or it. Extension can be determined in a thinking, by means of the thinking’s becoming-posited in it, without the thinking altering itself or it. Extension can be determined through an alteration (not of extension itself, *as extension*, but of the extended, material-containing), without the alteration, as alteration, altering itself or it as extension; (this is the plus of the formula under No. I). Alteration can [p. 159] be determined through an extension, without the extension altering itself or it; (likewise the plus of the formula under No. I). An extension determined merely through an alteration (not of extension itself, *as extension*, but of the extended, material-containing), without the alteration altering itself or it *as extension*, would be a certain determinate, for thinking as thinking ineradicable, hence, upon the positing of a thinking in it, just as it itself is ineradicable, so also in everything that would be thought in it (in everything − B in it) an ineradicable, i.e. necessary something, which would furnish nothing but the apodictic or necessarily-to-be-thought for a thinking. An alteration determined merely through an extension (without further possible alteration *pro eo, quod substat* [on behalf of that which underlies it], like the figures of geometry, or the conductors of my thinking in reading, the letters as well as numbers), without the extension altering itself or it, *as alteration*, would be a certain *determinate*, for thinking, *as thinking*, ineradicable, hence, upon the positing of a thinking in it, just as it itself is ineradicable, so also in everything that would be thought in it, (in everything − B in it) an ineradicable, i.e. necessary something, which would furnish nothing but the apodictic or necessarily-to-be-thought [p. 160] for a thinking. Herewith we would now have the apodictic completely out, not of geometry in general only, but *also of every particular geometrical figure*. Such a figure, e.g. the square of my table *as square*, is nothing other than an alteration determined as unalterable in an alterable (material-containing) something, *pro eo quod substat* [on behalf of that which underlies it], by means of extension *as extension*; in short, the square on this table is *its* substance, (exclusively furnished with sensible *attributes*, everything else on this table are *accidents*) or the square on it is the determinate, for thinking, as thinking, ineradicable, hence, upon the positing of a thinking in it, just as it itself is ineradicable, so also in everything that is thought in it, (in everything − B in it) ineradicable, i.e. necessary something, which furnishes nothing but the apodictic or necessarily-to-be-thought for a thinking. Both formulas above describe to me the analytic course of my thinking herein. I begin, namely, with No. II, (A + C) posited in (B − B + an alterable something); (this plus of the monstrating, e.g. the color or the cleanness, the size or smallness of the strokes in a geometrical figure, must, *as a matter* [p. 161] *of the monstrating*, be eradicated in thinking *when demonstrating*); and I end with the formula under No. I.

(A + C) posited in (B − B + a determinate something, inalterable for that which lies before the senses, of an alteration and extension without further possible alteration in that which just now lies before the senses, *insofar as it just now lies before the senses*).

That the first formula had to be set up merely for the sake of analysis, and that *its* plus, when it comes to thinking, becomes an eradicated plus, hence a minus, as the second formula shows, one sees without my reminding. But should this minus nevertheless not dawn here and there: then I have nothing further to do than to refer each one to his own becoming-conscious as a human being; how would it be possible that he could ever arrive even at this consciousness if the thousand-wheeled machine of which his physical being consists were not eradicated in his becoming-conscious, *as a becoming-conscious*, i.e. notwithstanding that it is the plus of his existence, did not become a minus in his consciousness, and if, insofar as he becomes conscious of himself as a human being, something else remained over besides the (A + C) [p. 162] posited in (B − B + a determinate inalterable something of an inner as well as outer impulse, together with all that which it, as impulse, brings with it *of the inalterable* from both sides)? But this is our formula under No. II, applied to a human becoming-conscious in general in its *specially* (no longer merely, as under the previous §., *generally*) considered activity. For what must necessarily be the case in every possible *animal* becoming-conscious, (as we considered the human under the previous §.), that the plus of the material, *of which the animal individual itself consists*, in its consciousness, *as consciousness*, becomes a minus of the material, that happens in an animal becoming-conscious *under a thinking* (under A), which is for that very reason called a *human* becoming-conscious, *through thinking, in everything that is to be thought, everywhere*; otherwise it is nothing thought.—But how, if now the plus of the alterable something of the second formula were in certain cases of such constitution that it came to one here and there in life first, perhaps only twice or thrice, though not in an equal and identical way, (this would be impossible in the material as material; *principium indiscernibilium* [principle of indiscernibles]), yet in a *similar* way, in a [p. 163] way, then, which is a consequence of our imagining-into the world, of our animal becoming-conscious, occurred; and our animal becoming-conscious recalled (through a faculty of memory) the second and third time that it occurred in a similar way, that alterable something of the second formula into itself: then we should perhaps have the advantage of subsuming absolutely under that first formula this second and third also, similar to that one, hence *of comprehending a manifold in One*, consequently also, for a whole row of similar apprehensions, of being able to *demonstrate out* everything that may follow absolutely from that first formula. The advantage is here touched upon only as a *Perhaps*, i.e. not yet as something thought, i.e. not yet as something furnished with its *prius*. Where, then, would a *prius* (no induction) be found for the extinguishing of the unphilosophical *Perhaps*? Had we for the similarity of our twice and thrice recurring case, as *this* similarity, thus as something merely left hanging in our sensible organs of conception, also yet an *equality* deriving from elsewhere: then that *Perhaps* would here be outlawed. But now the equality can lie in nothing but either in the form of thinking, or in the form of the material. What we seek is equality of a form for [p. 164] *our thinking*, not equality *of the form of our thinking*; hence we are, with the equality which is sought, referred first of all to the form of the material. Equality (identity) in the form of the material is equality in alteration, *as an alteration*, without further possible alteration *pro eo quod substat* [on behalf of that which underlies it], and is equality of extension, *as extension*, without further possible alteration of the extended, other than that which found itself enfolded in the first-posited plus of the alterable something of the second formula. Where, then, in the plus of a second and third alterable something (on beholding the second and third tulip, after we have seen a tulip the first time), our sensible organs of conception indicate similarity of the twice and thrice recurring case with the first, and there is added in the second and third case to that similarity equality in alteration, *as an alteration*, without further possible alteration, *pro eo quod substat*, and equality of extension, *as extension*, without further possible alteration of the extended, other than that which found itself enfolded (for a thinking) in the first-posited case of a certain alterable something, there our first formula fits not only for the first, [p. 165] but also for the second and third case, i.e. we now have a *concept*, or a complexion of sensible marks, indeed of a whole row of sensible apprehensions, which can all be demonstrated out apodictically from precisely the same plus of the first formula, without their being monstrated. But since the matter hereby begins to run out into the greater, the brevity needful for overview counsels, first of all, only a shorter formulation of our formulas themselves, but, in view of the importance of the subject, a recapitulation *as distinct as possible* of the essential points of our prius, so as to bring it as near to the capacity of each as can be done. As for the former, we can now, to that end, sum up what is found under

No. I. in the following letters

(A + C) posited in (B − B + b)

and what is found under

No. II. in the letters

(A + C) posited in (B − B + c)

together. (A + C) posited in (B − B + c) becomes for me in thinking = (A + C) posited in (B − B − c) i.e. in thinking = (A + C) posited in (B − B + b). From the *letters* tulip = (B − B + c) there arises in me the *thought* tulip [p. 166] = (B − B − c) = (B − B + b). If I then see a tulip again, and my organs thereupon call back into my animal becoming-conscious that which of the first remained hanging in them, and I find a *similarity* forced upon my animal becoming-conscious thereby: then I now have (A + C) posited in (B − B + c + c), and at the third return of the same case I have (A + C) posited in (B − B + c + c + c). But in thinking all these (+ c) persist only as One and precisely the same (A + C) posited in (B − B + b). How comes this?—I permit myself here first only to ask *analogically*: whether not also all objects in thinking persist only as One and precisely the same object? I conclude then further; but now my tulip is in thinking wholly and entirely an object; consequently it too will have to persist in thinking as One and precisely the same object. Only now again the difficult *This*: gets in one’s way; for the question is indeed properly no longer directed to the persistence of the (B − B), but of the (+ b) added thereto, as one and the selfsame (+ b), in all possible cases of its use.—Once one has done with the *This*: one has done with it once and for all, or else there was no doing with it; [p. 167] one missed the prius. A missed, i.e., as it is called, *false* prius (*no* prius) however does not hide itself: that is what *Kant* proves throughout, and proves it still near the end of his speculative career in his *animal* first principles of a doctrine of right, which he, to the mockery of reason, calls *metaphysical* first principles of the same. Our second concern must therefore now be a recapitulation, as distinct as possible, of the essential points of our prius, so as to bring it as near to the capacity of each as can be done; what for our (+ b) follows from it will then attach itself of its own accord:

A *as* A C In A, *through* A Outside-one-another, Juxtaposition-succession.

Through (A + C) arises an object = B − B.

In the object there arises a succession without further possible succession, other than the being-in-succession itself in the life-impulse, i.e. there arises an alteration without further possible alteration, or without all further alterability.

An alteration, (or an altered), without further possible alteration, without all further alterability, is one, into the state of [p. 168] privation, of the emptiness of an alterable, (into the state of a minus) transposed alteration.

An alteration, (or an altered), without further possible alteration, without all further alterability, is, however, also extension considered in itself and without adhering material (without an extended), for what can be altered in the extension is never it itself, *as extension*, but *the extended* as an extended something: if one therefore does away with this something: then one has in extension the only thinkable alteration without further possible alteration, or the only thinkable altered without all further alterability, after the material has been separated from it.

If we therefore have in the alteration without further possible alteration something transposed into the state of privation or emptiness, hence in this respect a minus: then we have on the other side in it also something positive, a *plus*, i.e. extension in itself and without adhering material.

Where, accordingly, it comes to an alteration without further possible alteration, as something *Negative* (a minus), there it comes at once [p. 169] to extension in itself and without adhering material, as something *Positive* (as a plus).

Where it comes to an alteration without further possible alteration as something Negative, as privation and emptiness of an alterable, there the alterable will *necessarily have to* attach itself, because otherwise it would come down to the ceasing of a succession, hence to the annihilation of a form, which, nonetheless, in the minus of an alteration without further possible alteration, could have prepared for itself nothing other than a means to the eternal continuation of its form under multiplications of the same in its unity.

Where it comes to an extension in itself and without adhering material, as something Positive, there extension *must* determine the alterable which attaches itself necessarily to that alteration without further possible alteration, just as it was determined beforehand through an alteration without further possible alteration, i.e. made into extension without something extended.

A juxtaposition and succession determined in an alteration without further possible alteration (after a self-returning [p. 170] motion) through extension, *as extension*, is called *organism*

If this happens through *the extended*, then one calls it *mechanism*.

In organism, accordingly, the form of the material multiplies or manifolds itself, without, *as form*, becoming or being anything other than it is, indeed without further variety (capability of multiplication) *as form*, except that which is ineradicably situated in the three dimensions of space, and *by means of this* is still possible in the material, *as material*. This gives the geometer his trichotomy, as the acknowledged pure basis of his science in angles, triangles, etc. Has the investigator of nature still to seek it for organism?—

In the object (B − B) organism (in its possibility) is therefore given to us; but the object itself has become for us only under a thinking (as immovable center). *Only under a thinking*, therefore, can organism also become; = (A + C) *as* A, *in* A, *through* A, posited in B − B + b; *purposes, ends, God!* (*Monas monadum* [monad of monads], *Ens realissimum* [most real being] with the immortal *Leibniz*; physico-theology).

[p. 171] Every plant is for thinking beings a revelation. That the sun is shining at present is a mere *in confesso est* [it is conceded], in the *eyes* of all human beings; a divine being is to me *apodictically* already revealed *with and at thinking*; (the objections which one will make against *this* on the strength of my above assertion, that thinking can posit nothing outside itself, I foresee; but I shall answer *such* objections with nothing, and merely ask: *whether one grants a certain fact, as fact*, or not? Fact is not philosophy, and philosophy not fact.

The form of the material itself is accordingly at once, *as form, the multiplicator of itself* under a thinking *outside us*, without altering itself as form in the least, and under a thinking *in us* this multiplicator gives itself to us as standing and equally inalterable, like the (B − B) itself, through a (+ b), which still comes to B − B, = 1 + 1 + 1 = 3. Now, with these three there, we have also come out completely at the individual and yet whole, which takes in all rows, as before at the possibility of the All of the rows in 1 + 1 = 2.—The variety in the unalterable, determined through [p. 172] an unalterable in the alterable material, *as material*, begins. The first *One* is the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, the second *One* is the *ground* (*fundamenti loco ponendum, s. id quod omnibus rebus substat, quatenus omnibus substat* [to be put in the place of a foundation, or that which underlies all things, insofar as it underlies all]), the third *One* is the *cause*; what the material, *as material*, supplies, are merely conditions (*conditiones*) for animal becoming-conscious, in order to return in a thinking to the cause, then to the ground, and finally to the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν. But the cause cannot be taken up into a thinking, *as a thinking*, i.e. *demonstratively cognized* (better: *cognized absolutely*), without the ground, the ground not without the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, (logic not without metaphysics). What one has therefore hitherto given out in philosophy as causes were not the *cognized cause*; (otherwise the *sufficiens*, as a shaming pleonasm, which even the language of life here and there avoided, would surely long since have been banished by philosophers too from the *principio rationis*; for *ratio* is either *ratio* or it is none, to assert a third, hence a *sufficientem rationem*, means not to understand oneself, in that one imagines oneself to have a principle and yet, with it, is stuck among [p. 173] the *conditionibus* of the material). What one has therefore hitherto given out in philosophy as causes were not the *cognized cause*; and what then? Answer: *a, inductively* raised to *omnitudo* or *universalitas* in similar cases, complexion of sensible (i.e., as I shall no longer be allowed to explain, *not yet purified of the material*) apprehensions in the empirical-persistent of an animal becoming-conscious, to which the *cause* (hence the + b) was lacking, in short, it was merely the sensible shell of the (+ b), under the name of *forces*, with which one began with the force of thinking and understanding; (the *Kantians* were here again subtle; only in their doctrines of the soul was there an understanding as *force*, in logic it was transformed into a *faculty* i.e. merely into the ground of the *possibility* of a faculty of cognition). For what, among the *conditionibus* for animal becoming-conscious, was found inductively, as *conditio sine qua non* for all others in similar cases, one calls *force* (force of feeling, force of sight, electricity), and then speaks in the end still of a *fundamental force*, as the *conditione sine qua non* for all preceding. But has one also already considered that this fundamental force either is no force any more, consequently also no fundamental [p. 174] force, or, if it is still a force, likewise again the shell around the kernel, i.e. a complexion, raised to *universalitas*, of sensible (still material-containing) apprehensions, hence a *petitio quæsiti* [begging of the question], without the (+ b) without the *cause*? (Has *Kepler* perhaps discovered the simple and general law of the heavenly motions *out of a force*, and when his pupil *Newton* founded his *general* attraction on the *Keplerian* discovery, was it this attraction, *as a force*, or was it not rather the *Prius*, once found by *Kepler*, upon which *Newton* went on to infer? No, the former it was not; for *Newton* himself indeed considered his attraction merely as a *phenomenon*, and this is precisely what we want here). Not the force, call it fundamental force or what one will, produces organism, but organism (the + b) produces the force, as it then reveals itself in an animal becoming-conscious, conditioned by the material. Mechanism cannot, without (in thinking) presupposed organism, be thought, any more than an extended without extension; (this latter proposition, by virtue of which mechanism presupposes organism, or, in other words, *mechanism through organism* [p. 175] *is first made possible*, I have long clung to so firmly, that I finally sent the much that I had written down about it to one of the greatest German naturalists for his inspection; now I can *demonstrate* it to myself in few words). How bottomless the confusion must become there, where one wants to apply *Kantian* philosophy to higher, i.e. prius-endowed, physics, imagines oneself to be seizing *principles*, and gropes about in the material and among intuitions, hence in all cases, (let one hang out one’s *world-soul sign* ever so promisingly, in place of the abandoned inn “*At the I*”), begins and ends with sheer force,—how bottomless, I say, the confusion must become *there*, is evident of itself. (A pity that the writer whom I mean here let the scholar in himself, for which he was born, be spoiled, and chose a career in which he is made merely for parroting and exaggerating, hence for paradoxes; in *this* region to bring it, by means of his own or foreign eyes, finally to half-seeing, is worse than being blind. Cf., e.g., what is asserted in the *Ideas for a Philosophy of Nature*, p. 128 ff., *on the origin of the concept of matter*, in particular p. 131, 139, 140, 141, and [p. 176] if a rational person finds a sense in it, I wish to have said nothing.)

This multiplicator, this standing unity in the many (in the variety of the form, possible through the three dimensions of space, without altering itself *as form*) is the *Facit*, become ours through our thinking, of a thinking *outside us*, which we now, as made, in a (+ b), into the *Facit* of a thinking *in us*, (in the animal I) and as thereby *understanding* nature, call our *understanding*, which gives us consequently, according to the doctrine of *thinking in general*, and according to the doctrine of the *object in general* or the *doctrine of primal division* (Ur-theilung), the plus mentioned above (§. 10. Rem.) to a *doctrine of understanding*, or to logic, so called hitherto. With omission of all other, not exactly very weighty, inferences, we turn therefore now merely again to logic, after its prius (its cause in the + b) has been completely (demonstratively) settled for it too, as logic so called hitherto, and the unalterability of this (+ b), as the necessary consequence of a form multiplying itself in the material (under A) without any alteration of itself, has been found.

We remained above at the (A + C) posited in (B − B + c + c + c), which becomes, *as thought*, [p. 177] (A + C) posited in (B − B + b). That is a threefold apprehension; this, one and precisely the same thought in a threefold apprehension, hence, *for the sake of the threefold apprehension*, a *concept* as thought. Into this concept *as thought* we then multiply also, furthermore, the fourth, fifth (+ c) etc., the fourth, fifth tulip etc., arrive thus by this way at an *omnitudo* of complexions of sensible marks under *one* (+ b), *one* thought, at the *genus*, in which we, in the *major* of a syllogism e.g., take up a certain sensibly given property of all tulips, in order to be allowed, in the *minor*, merely to undertake the *division* of the major with a single tulip, and then immediately in the conclusion to be able to *demonstrate that property upon it unseen*, provided that this sensible property in the alteration without further possible alteration, hence in the (+ b) of the tulip, as often as we yet saw a tulip, in our animal becoming-conscious (not in thinking) as *every-time* affection of the same (as an in all cases ineradicable impulse) at the sight of a tulip remained empirically persistently hanging, belonged consequently to the *sensible* attributes (*conditionibus, sine quibus non*) of the tulip, which, as above [p. 178] was said of my table, finally clung to its substance, hence in the first place also to the (+ b) applied to it, not as sensible *accidents* but as *their* sensible attributes, and are therefore also quite rightly regarded by us as the marks of their *cause*, *as a tulip*. The *species*, *subspecies*, *varietates* of tulips, on the other hand, are the *latitudo* described above in the manner of *existence* of a material, which, with the exception of the impulse in a *present* presence before us of a certain tulip, as an object of the senses, are eradicable in a tulip in general, hence belong to the *accidents* of the tulip, and make the logical proposition *absolutely cognizable* to me (*demonstrate*, not merely register inductively), that everything that holds of the *genus* (the universal) must also hold of the *particular* that stands under it, whereas what holds of the particular need not on that account also hold of the universal, i.e. that (in the order of our cognizing) *the accidents are necessarily determined by the attributes, but not the attributes by the accidents.* But the question is: what sort of sensible attributes will those be which, necessarily applicable to every tulip, cling empirically-persistently in my animal [p. 179] becoming-conscious of a tulip? 1) that the alteration without further possible alteration is, in it, bound to a certain *germ* (*genus remotissimum* of *organic bodies in general*, which must be included along in the *genus* of tulips as sensible attribute of each and every organic body) 2) that this germ presents the shape, size (*juxtaposition* in an extended something), mode of development (*succession* in an alterable something) of *plant germs in general* in my animal becoming-conscious (*genus remotius* of *plants in general*, which must be included along in the *genus* of tulips) 3) that this germ deposits roots and stalk, a calyx, stamens, anthers, a pistil and a stigma (*juxtaposition* in an extended something), a growth (*succession* in an alterable something), in my animal becoming-conscious, as flowers in general are wont to deposit all this therein (*genus proximum* of *flowers in general*, which must be included along in the *genus* of tulips), that finally 4) the ineradicable impulse, at each sight of a tulip, makes my animal becoming-conscious, in that which was named under No. 3, [p. 180] still further sensible differences noticeable, which are forced upon it nowhere else among flowers, but among tulips always; *genus* of tulips, determined by sheer complexions of sensible marks in animal becoming-conscious under the standing inalterable (+ b), which in us as outside us designates the *cause*, is called there *understanding*, as standing multiplicator, here *organism*, as standing multiplicator. This different naming, however, as will become sufficiently clear from what precedes, is a mere ἓν διὰ δυοῖν; for just that (A *as* A, *in* A, *through* A) *in us*, posited in C, gives us a (B − B) as object in general, and a (B − B + b) as *our* object, with the appendage of *our* life-impulse, *our self*, and gives us, after eradication of the material in similar beings outside us, an (A *as* A, *in* A, *through* A) posited in (B − B + b) to be cognized in similar beings *outside us*. I cannot be plainer; I believe it is enough *for an Outline*: every monad is a mirror of the universe, said the great *Leibniz*, admittedly still very poetically, — *and broke off*. Only the sensible attributes still require, for the time being, an elucidation before one can proceed to further inferences. For it seems as though we ourselves [p. 181] let one form be annihilated by the other, in that we, e.g., in thinking of a flower in general, expel the sensible attributes of the tulip genus from consciousness. It seems so; but is not so. I ask you, first, not to leave out of account the *sensible* in these attributes; and then, what is meant by a *sensible* attribute of a tulip? That which, at *each* apprehension of a tulip, returns in it as ineradicable impulse for animal becoming-conscious, and just on that account is made by us its *conditio, sine qua non*. Now, however, we already gave to the simplest impulse ineradicability for a thinking; consequently surely also to the repeated one. Only the impulse of another flower, in the *ineradicable succession of our representations*, whether this occur in the faculty of recollection or at the sight of it, is no longer the impulse of a tulip, as regards what is material-containing in the impulse, and where something, by virtue of the altered impulse in the *ineradicable succession of our representations*, as regards what is material-containing in the impulse, drops away of itself already, there it cannot, and hopefully need not, first be annihilated. More important than the removal of this easily foreseeable objection is what [p. 182] can be inferred from the attention hitherto paid to the sensible attributes, for the *demonstrative* constitution of syllogisms. That the *omnitudo* drops away in the object in general, as in thinking itself, has been shown. But it drops away also in the (+ b) considered in and for itself, and in sensible *attributes*, as its immediate clothing in a certain material, hence in the *genus*, or, where it is posited in this case, it merely takes the place of the *genus*, e.g. *all* tulips grow. The *genus*, or its representative in this case, the *omnitudo* (better: the *universality*), as exponent of the multiplication, already accomplished, of certain sensible apprehensions into a (+ b), constitutes, with its sensible attribute just now brought up, the one *terminus* (= x) of an (in empiricism) *demonstrative* syllogism, and gives the *dividendum*; the isolated apprehension (in the *minor*) constitutes the other *terminus* of the same (= y), and gives the *divisor* ( − B + b in x B in y ). The *terminus* of the universality of an attribute, cognized through ( − B + b in x B in y ), hence through the understanding posited in a [p. 183] certain apprehension, also as *terminus* of the individual (by virtue of the assertion of an All = so), is hence the essence of an (in empiricism) *demonstrative* syllogism. Where therefore the *terminus* of universality (x = − B) is an attribute = n, and the *terminus* of the individual (y = B) is an attribute = n, there the *terminus* of universality (x = − B) is also the *terminus* of the individual (y = B). Every (in empiricism) *demonstrative* syllogism rests accordingly *formally* merely on two *termini*, the *terminus* of the universal, cognized through an understanding posited in an apprehension, also as *terminus* of the individual. But how does it fare here with the old *Medius*, or, in our modern speech, indeed with the *middle concept*? It is, as *concept*, a pardonable excrescence of our previous *concept*- or material-logics, just as the *Kantian* concepts of reflection are, for a *Critique of Pure Reason itself*, an unpardonable excrescence of the same, in four pairs of new groping-machines of the human understanding, with which it grabs at the appearances and, with the assistance of the categories, seizes them right and left. Just look up the logics, and in particular the *Kantian* ones among them: one will become aware that there [p. 184] in the doctrine of concepts there is talk of a certain *Principio Indiscernibilium*, according to which two concepts that were altogether one and the same are not to be thought at all (*Jakob* l. c. p. 61). Now one becomes eager to learn how these logicians then explain 1) the *genus*, 2) the *Medius*? “A higher concept (see l. c. p. 64, cf. *Kiesewetter* l. c. pp. 32—35), which contains the common essential marks of a certain sphere of lower concepts, is called a *genus- or kind-concept*.”

Thus *common, essential* marks already in lower concepts, and these common essential marks then for the first time all together again in the genus-concept, hence surely also *common essential* marks of the genus-concept; and yet two concepts that were altogether one and the same are supposed to be an absurdity? That would plainly make the *genus* come to an absurdity. For the common essential marks are either identical or not. If they are identical, then there are identical marks, hence also identical concepts (*Jakob* l. c. p. 50 § 111), and the *principium indiscernibilium*, asserted a few pages before the *genus*, becomes a contradiction in respect of *concepts*. If they are not identical, then [p. 185] has *common* and *essential* no sense; for what is *common*? What is *essential*, if it is supposed to be found in the one entirely as in the other, and yet is not identical, consequently here so, there otherwise? Non-common, non-essential it is in this way, and yet is supposed to be common, and supposed to be essential, hence it is a contradiction. Can one therefore, if one will not expose oneself to any contradiction, which in the end means no more than if one will not deny one’s humanity, posit the common, the essential, the identity, in marks, *as marks*, in concepts, *as concepts*, or must not rather the identity and the essence be sought outside the marks and concepts, in the form, *as form*, hence in the inalterable of the impulse in general, as an alteration without further possible alteration in *momentary* apprehending, but, in the thought and in the *genus*, in (A *as* A *in* A *through* A) posited in (B − B + b)? As little as in the form, *as form*, a multiplicity takes place, just so little does there take place in the material, *as material*, an identity, hence a common, hence an essence: this *Leibniz* once proved inductively on tree leaves in a garden, which, with the greatest similarity, [p. 186] yet showed no equality. The *Kantian* concepts of reflection are accordingly 1) as *concepts*, which are supposed first to make an act of the understanding possible, although concepts presuppose a thinking and an understanding, as *facit* of thinking, for their very possibility 2) as *multiplicities* of a thinking even outside the table of thinking, beneath all critique. Sameness or non-sameness, agreement or disagreement, connected inwardly or outwardly, formally or materially (and as material *realiter*?) connected, and all that in thinking *as thinking*; (for the concepts of reflection no longer even relate to the determination of an *object*, as the categories do): this is the first philosophy *of its kind* under the sun. But, so as not to forget the *middle concept*, it is supposed to be one of the three principal concepts of a categorical syllogism, to be a mark of the subject, and finally the correct relation of the other two (of the subject and predicate) is supposed to be cognized through it (*Jakob* p. 112). *Kiesewetter* calls it (p. 104) simply the mediating representation, the intermediate mark of every categorical syllogism of reason. The *Medius* is thus here and there a concept, a representation, a mark, and belongs, *as this*, essentially [p. 187] to the possibility of a syllogism, i.e. to the cognition of the identity of x, as a universal, with y, as an individual. But now not even two concepts, two representations, two marks, that were altogether one and the same, can be thought (*Jakob’s* and *Kiesewetter’s Principium Indiscernibilium locis cit.*); consequently there can be cognized in no concept, *as concept*, no representation, *as representation*, no mark, *as mark*, the identity of x, as a universal, with y, as an individual, i.e. instead of becoming possible through a third concept, *as concept*, a syllogism through a third concept, *as concept*, becomes impossible, and all logicians who believed they *showed* the possibility of a syllogism through the *Medius*, *as concept*, have *demonstrated* through it, *as concept*, its impossibility. If I say: All planets move; the earth is a planet; therefore the earth moves: then the identity of the motion, as a universal, with the motion of the earth, as an individual, can absolutely not lie in the planet, as a concept, a representation, a mark; for there are indeed no concepts, no representations, no marks that were altogether one and the same; each planet, as [p. 188] concept, as representation, as mark, is rather itself something individual and different from the other planets; how can it, consequently, *as planet*, or as *concept* of a planet, mediate an identity? No, the identity lies rather in a non-concept, and indeed in the *All*, considered as a non-concept, in the representative of the *genus*, not as a complexion of sensible marks, but as a (+ b) situated still behind the sensible attributes, in the understanding, *as understanding*. The supposed *Medius* is our understanding itself, which, when in certain sensible attributes the *conditiones, sine quibus non* are sensibly given, takes up, as standing multiplicator, the form of the (material-containing) individual — a form by no means first determinable by those sensible attributes, but already conferred on it, *as understanding* — into the form of the universal, as altogether one and the same, and then, upon an occurring division of the universal by isolated apprehensions, recognizes the form of the universal again as (completely) identical with the form of the individual. In the formula

x = n / y = n x = y

that which gives the equation under the attribute n is our understanding, which the [p. 189] identity of form that has become known to it, *as understanding*, as a *facit* of thinking, — be it now clothed under a sensible attribute = x or = y or = n, be it shown under angles at the base or at the apex of a triangle, — recognizes as (completely) identical. It remains therefore thus, that the essence of an (in empiricism) demonstrative syllogism knows no *Medius*, but rests *logically* merely on ( − B + b in x B in y ); What, then, is the *Medius*, hitherto usurped for the possibility of a syllogism, e.g. in the above planet-medius? *Logically* the means of the sameness of two concepts in our cognition, and yet there are not two concepts that were one and the same, consequently *logically* a contradiction. *Sensibly* considered, on the other hand, it is an *index* or *exponent*, bestowed on us first through similarities in general, then through similarities in repeated apprehensions, hence through our progressive imagining-into (Hineinbildung) the world, an exponent of an identity in the form, an exponent which we ascribe *to ourselves* in the *omnis*, or, where the *terminus* of universality is already secured by the impossibility of a particularity, as in mathematics, simply make noticeable in letters; (for x is surely not [p. 190] y, and this n not that n, the angle at the apex of an equilateral triangle none of the angles at its base, as far as the *sensible* business of attributes in it is concerned: hence they too are mere exponents of an identity; (just bring two angles, however *equal* they may seem to the eye itself, under a magnifying glass; or judge whether the excellent *Blumenbach*, under the strongest possible magnification, in the origin of a water-moss, would have seen the *identity*). As to *similarity*, its discussion has so little difficulty at all that, properly understood, it offers itself to us of its own accord as *Medius*, and, since similarity takes place only in the material, will thereby cast the nativity of the *supposed logical Medius* once again. *Similar* is not, as one commonly says, when the sensible marks of the *one* object of the senses are also found in another: this cannot be; but when so many of the sensible marks of the *one* object of the senses drop away in apprehension that the *accidens* in it gives place to the sensible attribute, the sensible attribute to the (+ b), or to the no longer sensible identity, for the (unavoidable) connection with the sensibility before us.

[p. 191] *Similarity* rests therefore on a non-becoming-conscious, or no-longer becoming-conscious, of certain sensible marks, hence always on the minus of a becoming-conscious, already determined on each occasion by animality, with regard to the quantity and multiplicity of the material; every minus in the world is only minus in order to make room for a plus, (there is no negation that is not *relative*, no negation *as sheer negation*, in the world, the second course [*Gedeck*] at Germany’s table of thinking excepted): the minus of a becoming-conscious with regard to the quantity and multiplicity of the material (smaller *extension* of concepts in the logics) will therefore *have to* bring about the plus of a complexion, of a drawing-together of sensible marks, (greater *content* of concepts in the logics), which, as little as they can be identical, are just as little in consequence still (sensibly) separable from one another, (hence the firmness of concepts, as hitherto foundation of philosophy; they grow fast to one quite strongly); and the minus of a becoming-conscious with regard to all *sensible* marks, *as sensible*, but for that very reason *impossibly identical*, will *have to* bring about the (+ b) as identity, so as then first to let the sensible attributes in themselves (as a + b), [p. 192] the accidents however to the attributes (*quasi vero in gyrum res rediret* [as if the matter were indeed returning in a circle]), attach themselves in due order. What must be exceedingly helpful to the (+ b) for its easier manifestation here is, as goes without saying, either a minus of a becoming-conscious, accelerated in one by the particular kind of animality, with regard to the quantity and multiplicity of the material, (*Wit*, our ape-talent, still hovers close around the accidents, and is therefore manifold, and, where one can speak, exceedingly garrulous; — *Acuteness* is briefer; — *Profundity* briefest, it eradicates most material), or it is the frequent repetition of apprehensions in a certain section of the world under the standing unity of the (+ b), hence *experience*, only no *Kantian* one; hence the name of an *experienced* man in his field, when he has brought it to the husk of the (+ b), to the husk of the *cause*, in the sensible attributes (the sensible *Medius*) of *his* section of the world. — The *Medius* is dissected; further, then, to the essence of syllogisms *in general*, not only of the syllogisms *demonstrative* in empiricism; for now, after removal of the *Medius*, as a supposed ingredient of the (A + C *as* A *in* A *through* A) posited in (B − B + b), we have [p. 193] path thither. The x and y, as marks of the (in empiricism) *demonstrative*, likewise removed, the formula stands thus:

( − B + b B ) i.e. the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν (as A + C in − B), to which the *cause* is still added, divided by the ground (*per id, quod est fundamenti loco ponendum* [through that which is to be put in the place of the ground]). This must be the formula for the *demonstrative* syllogisms in that which one nevertheless knows with certainty without empiricism, without previously made experiences, without routine. (− B + b) is called a *possibility* (posited through A + C), or an applied *thinking* (posited through A + C), to which a *cause* or *substance*, consequently an extension which (under an A) has become the multiplicator of itself, was still added. The B placed beneath is called, as B, the *actuality*, insofar as it is taken up into a thinking, or the *ground*, hence extension *as extension*, without further possible alteration of the extended. The B placed beneath, as divisor of the (− B + b), is called accordingly the extension, *as extension*, without further possible alteration of the extended, insofar as it is contained in a *cause* or *substance*, which is the product of a thinking out of that extension [p. 194] is. The extension, *as extension* without further possible alteration (under an A), is *life* in general, which, insofar as the extension, *as extension*, is contained in a cause or substance, manifests itself as multiplicator of itself in the multiplicity of the material (plant life, animal life), and in the − B, in man, has taken a thinking to itself. ( − B + b B ) will therefore be called, *life in general, contained in a substance, to which the imperative of thinking itself still was added.* This is man; he in his own person has thus become our first *consequentia immediata*, in that in that formula we sought only the *consequentias immediatas* that he, as man, is wont to make. For a test, therefore, take it up once here also with the above scales, throw away the (+ b), found in the meantime, superfluous for *this* test, and set what stands on those scales under ( − B B ): so one has

( − B B ) as an impulse of animal life, contained in man still in an imperative.

[p. 195] ( − B B ) as drives, contained in man still in an ought.

( − B B ) as a desiring, contained in man still in a willing.

Etc. But apply the same − B B to the theoretical too, and the same thing comes out again, according to the following scales:

B − B

Impulse of animal life Imperative

Apprehension A thinking, considered according to the *possibility* of its application.

Apperception A thinking, considered according to the *actuality* of its application.

Representation A thinking, considered according to the *necessity* of its application.

Concept / Judgment / Syllogism } A thinking, considered according to the identity of its application to whole complexions of sensible apprehensions.

[p. 196] ( − B B ) will therefore, according to the second row of these scales, be an apprehension, contained in the possibility of a thinking, i.e. in a human being. According to the last three rows, ( − B B ) will be a concept, a judgment, a syllogism, as complexions of sensible marks, contained in the identity of a thinking. Partly to (− B), partly to (+ b), partly to (B), all *consequentiæ immediatæ* must now also refer, everything must refer that man knows with certainty *without empiricism*; for of ( − B + b B ) he himself consists. (− B) gives the *consequentias immediatas* of the *doctrine of reason*, i.e. of thinking, *as a thinking*, insofar as it comprises knowing as well as acting; i.e. (− B) gives the *postulates under* (− B), which *Kant* misused, *per syntheses a priori*, into unheard-of, and hence in fact new, multiplicities in thinking itself. There is and can be only *One* postulate for *truth, virtue, right*, and hence for *logic, morals, natural right*, *One* postulate, *One consequentiam immediatam*, of which all others (under − B), as *its consequentiæ immediatæ*, or better: as various [p. 197] expressions of it, according to the multiplicity of occurring cases, must in turn issue forth.
