This postulate is a *consequentia immediata* of our thinking, demonstrative without empiricism, as *an* imperative, and reads: Everything that the impulse from within and the impulses from without (sensations and representations) bring to you (hence present to a *human* working-over), let that lie with you not merely as it has been brought to you, according to the course of time, customary manner, and current delusions (*permettes moi, que je vous endoctrine* [allow me to indoctrinate you], says *Voltaire*’s confessor), according to the sequence of *your own* association, according to impulses of *your* passions, but rather efface this (*yours*) from it and take it into a thinking! In short, *Cogita* [think]; promote everything from the first also to the second scale, from that under (B) also to that under (− B), so as then first to let that under (B) attach itself again to that under (− B); *therefore* you are man, and in that you have to perform this *as man*, you commit a contradiction, dehumanize yourself, if you do not perform it. *Commit therefore no contradiction, neither in your cognition, nor in your manner of acting*; this is the universal postulate under the imperative of your humanity, [p. 198] fruitful in sheer *consequentiis immediatis* (under − B). In that you, e.g., will that one not insult *you*, you will that one insult *no human being*; but if you nevertheless insult someone, you fall with your willing into a contradiction. *Willing, under an imperative desiring, demanding*, you can namely do only *as man*; (setting aside Kantian-absolute freedom, and dignity and end in itself, and whatever the thing in itself may be called). In that you, then, can will *only as man*: so what you will holds for humanity; non-insult of humanity you therefore demand in the demand that one should not insult you; if you nevertheless insult: so you fall, as said, into a contradiction; (what you will that people not do to you, that do not do to them either; and what you will that people do to you, that do to them also; only the difference prevails here, that the *ought-to-do*, *officia humanitatis* [duties of humanity], can be *constituted* only through the preceding taking-up of a *certain* case into a thinking, hence empirically, regulated it is always; the *ought-not-to-do*, however, stands in advance for all cases under the proposition of contradiction). If you fall into a contradiction, you can with nothing more [p. 199] excuse yourself (your *conscience*); for only the human being can excuse himself, and the human being has slipped away from you in your contradiction. If he has slipped away from you, then you are in this case an *animal* (in spite of all your materiature in concepts, judgments, syllogisms); the security against offenses, which your humanity gave you precisely by the fact that *it* could be offended and, as you yourself will, should not be offended—this security is gone; you are an animal, and without offending yourself in this regard (this is no longer possible for *this* case), one can now punish you, for one punishes merely the animal in you (together with the materiature of *your* concepts, judgments, syllogisms), since you yourself did after all want to be a human being, and hence one punishes the contradiction in your essence, which in other words is called *forgetfulness of duty*, *wrong*. Only where you do no wrong, violate no duty, and hence have right, can one offend you. Now, however, you violate no duty, e.g., when you occupy an ownerless thing (*rem nullius*), because it is *res nullius*; you therefore do right in occupying it, and whoever might perhaps subsequently want to disturb you, since you violated no duty with your occupation but did right in it, would *offend you*, and would, as could easily be [p. 200] shown, fall into a contradiction with that which he nevertheless himself wills; etc.—Nothing but immediate inferences under (− B), when, with actions, it comes *to thinking*; whence it looked, before the *Kantian* epoch, though one might almost say *outside* many schools and lecture-chairs generally, still so clear when one had to *convince* a person, even a quite young one, that this was right, that wrong. It really seemed to one that the lad knew all this already, before one told him it through *synthetic* postulates *a priori*; it came to him as if on a string.—*Duty and right* are nothing other than the identity of our thinking itself, posited in certain representations and concepts. Just this is *truth*; the same *consequentia immediata* of thinking as a thinking (in − B), which pronounces for duty and right: *commit no contradiction*; is also the universal postulate for truth. Here, however, there manifests itself anew the whole weakness of our hitherto so-called *theoretical* philosophy. In *actions* our thinking set itself, *as a thinking*, as an eternal norm for each of us, pressed in the momentary case of action for a *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν for every action that was just then in progress, [p. 201] for the eradication of the *conditionum, sine quibus non* in the material, of the sensible attributes *in us* (of the temperament), in order to let this sensible be joined again under its sovereignty, for the immediate working of the (A *as* A, *in* A, *through* A) into the *ground* (into extension, as extension, without further possible alteration of the extended, hence into *life in general*), and into the *cause* (into the extension multiplying itself in itself under one thinking, or the substance *in us*, hence into *our life*). Here, in *actions*, the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν (through thinking in *conscience*) was thus demanded, lay there in plain day, as demanded of the most common human beings, in order *to be a human being*, and to have seen in this, lying there in plain day, what he saw in it, characterizes the *Kantian* acumen quite as advantageously on the one side as the manner in which he dealt with that fortunate view puts his *speculative* talent, his capacity for consistency, in short thinking in its application in him, in a disadvantageous light. To arrive with that view at thinking itself as well was, namely, by virtue of the vivacity of his genius and the originality of his combining wit, no longer possible for him; and he therefore had to mistreat, in the most unseemly manner, the [p. 202] thinking, *as a thinking*, or pure speculative reason, as he calls it, tear it away, as inadequate to his own purposes, as willing and not able, as dialectical, from the practical (which can, because it wills?), and misguide the practical itself, insofar as he saw the *prius only in this* (*in freedom*), perversely enough, until it finally, in the consistent *Fichte*, issued in an I with all its new splendors, which were besides imparted to the *Fichtean* consistency by hasty memory-heads without *Forbergian* taste and without *Forbergian* humor.—Instead of uniting everything, *Kant* tore everything apart, in order, under the magic wand of his mighty genius, to become the new *synthetic* creator of everything. And yet precisely that which our thinking declares in a *conscience* must everywhere be its universal declaration. Beyond the *conditiones sine quibus non* in the material, to grasp the cause, as *cause*, the ground, as *ground*, the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, as *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, in one thinking, and to cognize cause and ground, with their unchangeableness *as this*, yet in the changeable of the material that stands under them as wholly dependent on that prius, and consequently [p. 203] finally to subject all in all to this prius—*this is just as much the goal of all speculation as it is the daily task of our conscience*. But where was *speculative* philosophy hitherto which had brought it (without images) beyond the *conditiones sine quibus non*, even so far as the cause? Yet we come to the cause (+ b) and the ground (B) only later: the *theoretical consequentiæ immediatæ* under (− B) must first have been completed. Where, then, was speculative philosophy hitherto which had brought it to thinking, *as a thinking*, and hence to the *theoretical consequentiis immediatis* under (− B)? Just look at its very first inferences in speculation! The law of identity, hence thinking itself, was supposed to be an immediate consequence of the principle of contradiction; did one there understand thinking as a thinking? The principle of exclusion was supposed to flow absolutely from thinking, as a (pure) thinking; for it was after all found everywhere among the supreme laws of thought, and indeed here and there with the express direction that it proceed from the *pure* thinking in the principle of contradiction? immediately. From our thinking, as a pure thinking, there followed, then, already an *Either*—or, followed already that [p. 204] to every object a certain mark (something sensible) either belongs or does not belong; for that is how the principle of exclusion was hitherto understood. The *principium rationis* (our *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν) (A as A *through* A), which is one with the law of identity (A as A in A), was treated as a sensible *conditio sine qua non*, hence furnished with the addition of a *sufficiency*, and then nevertheless inferred absolutely from it: the ground, *already as ground* (our *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν), posits its consequence necessarily. From thinking, as a *pure* thinking, there followed, then, already a *hypothesis* (of sufficiency), and yet again no hypothesis, insofar as the ground already as ground (our *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν) posited its consequence *necessarily*. With such *consequentiis immediatis* under (− B) the hitherto prevailing speculation began. No, the *consequentiæ immediatæ* of our thinking, *as a thinking*, under (− B), of the (A as A in A through A), are rather that it admits just as little as does the imperative of our conscience any difference of quantity, quality, modality, or relation, besides what has hitherto been remarked on this. Now to the *consequentiis immediatis* under (+ b), when it has first been settled [p. 205] whether we shall not perhaps have to take up the (B) even before the (+ b), with its immediate inferences, in accordance with our *present* purpose. (+ b) itself is a consequence of thinking when it, posited in the material, eradicates this in thinking, having thereby found an object = (B − B), but having also won for the object the genus for an: *this* object. (+ b) is therefore preceded by an (A as A in A through A), posited in (B − B), and accordingly we shall have to proceed first to the (B), without regard to the placement of the above formula ( − B + b B ), in accordance with the order and with our *present* purpose. Which, then, are the *consequentiæ immediatæ* under (B)? (B) represents to us 1) extension, *as extension*, without further possible alteration of the extended in the object (under an A). Whatever, therefore, holds of extension, *as extension*, (under an A), such as the axioms and postulates of geometry, will belong to the *consequentiis immediatis* under (B). (B) represents to us 2) in alteration without further possible alteration of the alterable (under an A) *time*. Whatever, therefore, holds of time, *as time*, (under A), will belong to the [p. 206] *consequentiis immediatis* under (B). (B) represents to us 3) as extension without further possible alteration of the extended (under an A), organism and life *in general*. Whatever, therefore, holds of organism, *as organism*, of life, *as life in general*, such as, e.g., the sympathy of the whole living nature (that overflowing into the fellow-feeling of a creature, *Goethe*), that will belong to the *consequentiis immediatis* under (B). (B) represents to us 4) the ground, *id quod est fundamenti loco ponendum* [that which is to be placed in the position of a foundation], (under an A). Whatever, accordingly, holds of the ground, *as ground*, consequently is an *essentiale consecutivum* of extension and motion (under an A) in geometry as well as in mechanics and chemistry (water in *Thales*, *air* in *Anaximenes*, *ether* in *Pythagoras*, light-material in more recent times, in short a *fluid as life-fluid*), that will belong to the *consequentiis immediatis* under (B). Without empiricism we know with certainty how man is to use the mechanism of his hands and feet: without empiricism there are born *mechanical* and born *philosophical* heads; to the former the *consequentiæ immediatæ* under (− B) are, to the latter the *consequentiæ immediatæ* [p. 207] under (B) readily present, without their necessarily having, in this, to see into the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν as well. This leads us to the *immediate* inferences under (+ b). The difficult thing: *This*: on the object compelled us, out of our (B − B), to the addition of a (+ b). We ran, in going out, upon a multiplicity without possible survey of its limits, and all this multiplicity, ourselves not excepted, becomes for us here a *This*, there a *This*, and again a *This*; for us, therefore, it becomes *identical* and *one*, that which in a multiplicity, without possible survey of its limits, ourselves not excepted, lies before our apprehensions. Identical and one can be nothing that belongs to the material, *as material*; identical and one is the *genus* (+ b): the *genus* can therefore not belong to the material. If it does not belong to the material, as *in* the material, it belongs to the *form*, as *on* the material; for *on* the material the genus gives itself. The form becomes for us under a thinking; under a thinking there becomes for us, therefore, the genus: this thinking in a genus we call our *understanding* (+ b). But our understanding can no more than our thinking make anything where there is nothing. Identical and one, where our understanding cognizes it, genus, [p. 208] where our understanding cognizes it, must therefore have a *being* as *identical and one*, and as *genus*; otherwise our understanding could not cognize it. To have a being, and yet not to consist in thinking itself, and yet to be called no longer an object absolutely, but *this* object, leaves one no other choice than to let the form of the material multiply or manifold itself, *as form*, under an A (+ b as extension without further possible alteration of the extended, which multiplies itself under an A), and then to let it take up into itself the manifold of the material, (+ b, as *life individualized in one material*, which, however, as (+ b) presents the form of the universal, i.e. of extension under (B), in the particular exactly as in the universal, hence every *This* is a genus, and every genus a *This*). The extension multiplied in itself under an A is the *cause* (+ b, as cause) *quæ singulis rebus substat pariter ac omnibus* [which stands under individual things as under all alike]; (+ b as substance of the individual beings). That which (+ b) represents would therefore be the *This* in its identity and unity, = genus = understanding = extension without further possible alteration of the extended, as it *multiplies itself in genus and understanding*, [p. 209] = *individualized life*, considered according to the *possibility* of its being individualized, = form of the universal in the particular or individual, = cause = substance (*quatenus singulis rebus substat pariter ac omnibus* [insofar as it stands under individual things as under all alike]). The *consequentia immediata* of (+ b) as a *This* in its identity and unity is that we can speak, write, and read; for the *being able*, the *possibility* of it, rests on no empiricism; in all three cases the sensible attributes of the material *as material*, up to the impulse from without, must be eradicated in thinking, as thinking, in order to arrive at the *This* in its identity and unity through thinking: German is in this respect like French, and French like English, etc. The *consequentia immediata* of (+ b), as a *This* in its identity and unity, is also the persistence of our *self-consciousness in general* (which, taken together with its sensible attributes described above, or *conditionibus sine quibus non*, fixes our individuality, as that of a *person*, hence the *dear* I). The *consequentia immediata* of (+ b) as a *This* in its identity and unity is, further, the *possibility* of a conviction (I say: *conviction*) through *reminiscences* [p. 210] etc. The *consequentiæ immediatæ* of (+ b) *as a genus* are 1) the union of every mark with the concept in which it is contained or represented in juxtaposition and succession, as also of every predicate with its subject in a judgment, the judgment being affirmative or negative, insofar as this judgment and that concept are considered *logically*, hence in every case the identity of the form of the universal (in the predicate, or there in the concept) with the form of the particular (in the subject, or there in the mark of the concept) is asserted therein; 2) the traditional propositions of logic, *propositiones singulares æquipollent universalibus* [singular propositions are equivalent to universal ones]; what holds of the universal holds also of the particular which stands under it; what holds of the particular *taken together* holds also of the universal (*induction*), etc.; 3) the possibility of a conviction through analyzed concepts (without a present apprehension) insofar as one thereby gives someone to recognize the identity and unity of the universal also in the individual, consequently also the possibility of bringing someone by admonitions to the point that he must think himself without delay as comprehended under a universality (a rule or a law). The *consequentiæ immediatæ* of (+ b) *considered as genus*, [p. 211] are also the *consequentiæ immediatæ* of (+ b), *considered as understanding*. The *consequentiæ immediatæ* of (+ b), as an *individualized life*, or as the form of the universal in the particular, as it occurs in the life of every human being, *as an individual being*, are that he *knows* (not merely feels) what is conducive to the needs of his life, insofar as he *knows* this (drive of self-preservation: in man *self-love*). The *consequentiæ immediatæ* of (+ b) as a cause, or substance, are the identity of the form of the universal with the form of the particular in respect of the *peculiar* character, the *peculiar* temperament, the *peculiar* talents of a human being, etc., further the (hypothetical) necessity of that which is cause or substance, under (B), and of the (B) under (A) *as the only absolute*, or the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν. So much for the *consequentiis immediatis* (or the various expressions for the same) from ( − B + b B ) as that of which man consists. The *consequentiæ immediatæ* from ( − B + b B ), however, are *syllogisms*, and these syllogisms have nothing pure-demonstrative [p. 212] about them, than precisely this ( − B + b B ) itself. The *only pure-demonstrative* thing in syllogisms will therefore be ( − B + b B ); now, however, precisely this is found also in judgments and concepts. The *only pure-demonstrative* thing of syllogisms (or, as one has hitherto said, the *logical* essence of syllogisms) is therefore also the *only pure-demonstrative* thing of judgments and concepts. It is called ( − B + b B ), and is the *analysis* in its simplicity in everything and anything cognizable. This leads us occasionally, before we go further, to a brief survey of the *pure-demonstrative* in all sciences in general.

Philosophy, 1st Part. / (A as A, in A, through A.)

Doctrine of magnitude in general / (A as A, in A, through A) posited in (B).

Philosophy, 2nd Part. / (A as A, in A, through A) posited in (− B)

Doctrine of magnitude applied to individual magnitudes.

[p. 213] Philosophy, 3rd Part. (A as A, in A, through A) posited (in B + b)

(A as A, in A, through A) posited in (− B + b)

## *Transition to the Doctrine of Nature.*

(+ b)

(Here only then — / the sensible *attributes* / —— *Accidents* / attach themselves again.)

It is quite impossible to have a concept, to pass a judgment on anything in the system of the world, to make a syllogism, without there lying at its ground

(B) contained in (− B + b),

otherwise it is no concept, no judgment, no syllogism. *Pure-demonstrative*, however, is that without which it would not be possible at all to have a concept, to pass a judgment, to make a syllogism; the rest must be demonstrated *Kantianly*, i.e. one must point to it *with fingers* [p. 214] until it arrives with it (in a c + c + c +), hence in recurring similarities of view, at the cognizing of its sensible attributes. The sensible *attributes* now, as *sensible medii*, are banished above to their proper place; the essence of the syllogism has, after the clearing away of the former, just been set forth, and it seems, therefore, that we are done. (*Veritas limata est in disputatione* [truth is polished in disputation], Off. II, 10). We would be, if mere *accidents* could not also be taken up into syllogisms, and thereby be snatched from undemonstrative finger-pointing. So at least it seems to one; it must therefore be investigated. That mere accidents really can also be taken up into syllogisms is proved, besides daily common experience, by the venerable precedent of the father of critique, who declares judgments that *without the mediation of a third representation* can be derived from another, hence the *consequentias immediatas* from ( − B + b B ), consequently the *pure-demonstrative* in our whole cognition, to be *mere syllogisms of the understanding*, but those syllogisms, on the other hand, *which still need another judgment* in order to effect their consequence, to be genuine *syllogisms of reason*? [p. 215] The example with which he elucidates this *Critical* discovery is all too apt for our purpose for us not to retain it entirely, as it is communicated on p. 360 of the *Critique of Pure Reason*. When from the major premise: *All men are mortal* (− B + b in x), it is inferred: *some men are mortal* ( − B + b in x B in y ): then this is, in *Kant*, a *consequentia immediata*, and a *consequentia immediata* is called by him a *mere syllogism of the understanding*. The law of identity, applied under (+ b) to a sensible attribute, is thus for him a *consequentia immediata*, and *this consequentia immediata* would then be the *mere syllogism of the understanding*. Insofar as, in the given case, the form of the universal were also immediately cognized as form of the particular, hence the x and y, or the sensible attributes first brought out by observations, as here the attribute of mortality, were thought away, he would have done well to call this then a *consequentiam immediatam*; but precisely thereby it would now be no mere syllogism of the understanding any more; but the *pure essence* of every syllogism in *applied* thinking, hence in *reason* itself. But since he in the present [p. 216] example expressly draws a sensible attribute, that of mortality namely, into its *consequentiam immediatam*: then it is no longer a *consequentia immediata*, but merely a syllogism that is *demonstrative* in empiricism, i.e. through observations which finally lead to sensible attributes (induction), and through sensible attributes, *insofar as they attach themselves* to a (+ b), hence no longer a pure-demonstrative syllogism, no longer a syllogism in its *pure* essence, to which one would therefore, as a syllogism afflicted with sensible attributes, whose sensible attributes however attach themselves immediately to (+ b), sooner give the name of a *syllogism of the understanding* than of a *consequentia immediata*. But these are trifles, which entailed for the *Kantian* logics no other inconvenience than that, e.g., *Kiesewetter* believes he is dealing for twenty-six pages (pp. 72–98, l. c.) with *consequentiis immediatis*, where he is dealing with demonstrative syllogisms *from attributes in empiricism* under (+ b), hence with ( − B + b in x B in y ), that he 2) *genuinely Kantianly* regards every *consequentiam immediatam*, apart from the false sense which he gives it, merely as the forecourt to syllogistic, by no means as the *proper essence* of every syllogism, and that he [p. 217] 3) presses these two incorrect presuppositions, like every article of his logic, through sheer differences of quantity, quality, relation, and modality, as putative forms of the purest thinking, once more to the point of satiety. But the worst case is that of the *Critique*, l. c., when it now proceeds further in its above example, and thereby also quite completely gives out its opinion of the *actual syllogisms of reason*, beyond possible misunderstanding, clear as the sun. It runs there as follows: “On the other hand the proposition: all scholars are mortal, does not lie in the underlying judgment (of the mortality of men), for the concept of scholars does not occur in it at all, and it can be inferred from this only by means of an intermediate judgment.” If, accordingly, one goes out from the sensible attributes, and along them, even all the way to the mere *accidents*, in order to take these up under (+ b) into a representation for a thinking, if, that is, one connects sensibly the most matter that one can bring together in one’s sensibility, in order to take up into a representation for thinking whatever qualifies itself for this: then there reveals itself to the Critique [p. 218] *only here*, consequently in the greatest quantum of the material, the essence of *actual syllogisms of reason*. How things stand here again with their thoroughness, with their presumed glance into the depths of our essence, others may judge. I do not trouble myself now either about this, or about the crookednesses which have thereby passed into our newest logics; but pursue merely my own, as yet uncompleted, path up to the point where it must finally also instruct me completely about the relation of the sensible *accidents* to my thinking. It is perhaps the most difficult task that I still have before me; but at least this reassures me in it, that it reveals itself to me, quite as difficult as it is, unreservedly. My above formula namely grows now on to a ( − B + b in x + c B in y + c ) i.e. to a *Kantian* actual syllogism of reason, e.g. all *double* (c) tulips are rare, now however this tulip is double;—and this c, this mere accident, I can nevertheless use in no thinking, *as thinking*. This intermediate judgment of a *Kantian* proper syllogism of reason (in c) must, as accidental, for a thinking, *as thinking*, again be removed, so much I see; but whither with it, and [p. 219] what then is put in its place in thinking, since I already have the (+ b) in the formula? An analogy occurs to me here first of all again.—I know from the language of life, and the *Critique* has proved it to me, that I can apply the thoughts of possibility, actuality, and necessity, which properly belong only to the object (= B − B) in me essentially and unchangeably, also arbitrarily to mere accidents of existence, being-there, of appearing or vanishing before my eyes, indeed that with these accidents I can alternate them, and e.g. call something that, *as object*, must always be possible, possible only when it moves into my field of vision, something that as object must always be actual, actual only when it moves still nearer into my field of vision, something that as object must always be necessary, necessary only when it lies palpable before me, or also, with respect to its accidental absence, can call it necessarily absent only when it has withdrawn itself wholly from my palpation and my other senses. Since the Critique has proved this to me irrefutably in measuring out my modality, the question arises: whether I might not now take the liberty of trying it likewise with [p. 220] my (+ b) *as genus*, and hence of applying the (+ b) to mere accidents too, where, to be sure, strictly speaking, *as with accidents*, there is no genus, and, strictly speaking, as with accidents, no concept either (as a *sensible* attribute of the + b) any more. The standing multiplicator is once and for all at hand. Why, then, should I not be allowed to multiply everything that comes, be it genus or non-genus, concept or non-concept, attribute or non-attribute, by means of it, indeed in the end perhaps even *have to*? In this way I arrived at a (+ b) in mere *species, subspecies, varieties*, in short in accidents; I arrived at concepts where, *as with accidents*, there are strictly speaking no concepts any more in that sense of the word according to which one could demonstrate to me with apodictic certainty a whole series of sensible apprehensions straight from the complexion of its sensible marks under a (+ b), but where one would always, *as with accidents*, still have to bring a pointing of the finger into play, such as, say, the concept of a region, of a landscape, of the starry heaven (in *astrognosy*), and all those concepts that assert nothing *organic*, for which I have consequently merely borrowed the multiplicator, as for my table [p. 221] and my chairs, as so-called concepts. This would give me then a geography, a topography, a technology, etc., under a (+ b), whereby, however, a pointing of the finger would always have to do duty as the indispensable servant for my (+ b). Would this give me the fair world of fictions, of these accidental configurations, which are accidental and yet not *without rule*? — Under (+ b) they join, like the accidental and indemonstrable, but otherwise often very meaningful element of human dreams, onto thinking in man. — Their standing multiplicator under an A, (the ἓν κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, not ἓν καὶ πᾶν, the *unchangeable*, *eternal*, before whom I worship in this husk of material, which I owe to him) was held out to me by nature in the organism; therefore I now also *comprehend* the organism: it is the construction of nature outside me, just as it is constructed in me under an A; here I find myself at home in *proper concepts*; for I can *demonstrate* from concepts. But the dust is harder to comprehend than the flower; this the *Stagirite* already remarked, and I feel it *now*. However, either my previous prius was no prius, or in that which I have asserted hitherto there must also have been enveloped what [p. 222] I now still seek. To the material I ascribed multiplicity, to the form of the material unity in its plurality, and plurality in its unity. In three indestructible dimensions of space the form of the material spreads out into the ramifications of its unity, hence into its plurality, into a plurality which, as indestructible as the form everywhere, 1) under an A (in B − B) becomes the multiplicator of itself (+ b), without in the least altering itself (three is in six what it is in three hundred and twenty-three), which 2) takes all that comes to it of material into an alteration without further possible alteration under an A (B − B), and because it happens under an A, and *can happen only under one A*, the *succession*, as second form of the material, can now be made to describe its circular orbit in itself just as regularly as unalterably. This the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν (A), this the ground (B), this the cause (− B + b); and indeed both the latter in their *peculiar*, but also in their *relative* determinations *to* A, and their *absolute* ones *through* A. The whole basis we have therefore now, and have it as briefly together as possible for our purpose. But the material is not simple like all this; it is *manifold*, not identical. From [p. 223] the material and on the material itself, *as material*, *variety* will therefore be taken up, into this and that unity once *absolutely* determined under A in the plurality of the form, as into the *ground* (*fundamenti loco ponendum*) and as into *its* cause (under A); consequently *variety* too can be promoted, in an alteration without further possible alteration, to the regular succession, to identity, as the ground of its alterations, insofar as it is something alterable, i.e. material. One need only recall, e.g., the variety in colors and tones on the material. This variety gives us our *accidents*. But this variety, *as accidens* considered by us, is taken up into the ground and into the cause (under A) just as well as a sensible attribute; it will therefore be as necessarily determined by ground and cause (under A) as ever a sensible attribute can be determined by this and that (*nihil sine ratione*). The superfluous (+ c) of our formula, the *Kantian* syllogism of reason, *as syllogism of reason*, is therefore expunged; ( + c + c ) is = ( x y ), and ( x y ) we have already above removed from the syllogism, *as syllogism*; [p. 224] consequently our formula now stands for ever, as long as one will still draw syllogisms in the human world, secured and pure, as the essence of every syllogism, which is called ( − B + b B ). Here I remark merely in passing that for the possibility of this formula, hence for the essence of every syllogism, as of every judgment and concept, a disjunction in (B − B) is already presupposed, and that the ill-treated proposition of *exclusion*, which asserts the former, instead of already drawing marks into it, would have to be expressed thus: *apart from possibility and actuality there is nothing true in itself downward in the row and into physical life*; but that the *hypothetical* syllogisms are quite properly the syllogisms for *accidents*; consequently can absolutely never, as *hypothetical*, belong to the *consequentiis immediatis*, as *Kiesewetter* seems to have discovered (l. c. p. 73). But how is one then to understand this, that accidents, which are given out as equally necessary with the attributes in regard to ground and cause (under an A), and *proved* as equally necessary in regard to ground and cause (under an A) as these, nevertheless and for all that remain *accidents*? Answer: as little as [p. 225] matter can pass over into a thinking, *as thinking*, *or let itself be transformed into it*, just as little can the prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, the ground and the cause, pass over into an apprehension, *as apprehension*, or, (*as was only now to be explained*) *let itself be transformed into it*. Where neither can be transformed into the other, and yet the one acts on the other, there the one merely arouses the activity of the other, or the one becomes impulse for the other. Apprehensions can therefore become impulse for the prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, the ground and the cause, consequently also for thinking *in its application* in us; but the prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, the ground and the cause, consequently thinking *in its application* in us, will also be able to determine *present* apprehensions in us; (without their, however, considered as *copulae*, i.e. *formaliter*, altering themselves in the least, i.e. canceling or destroying themselves as *copulae*.) If an apprehension in a given case is stimulating or determining (impulse, *conditio, sine qua non*) for the activity of the applied A in us at all: then we push, in the language of life, the possibility, now arising, of a certain making-use of thinking in us, [p. 226] upon the sensibly present object, and say, it is *possible that it is this or that*, i.e. I can now think something, an object = (B − B), in connection with it; the making-use of thinking at all, now arising in us for a present case, therefore concresces us here in phantasy with the object of sense, *as object of sense* (with C); we are born *Kantians*. But if the object of sense moves closer into our field of vision, and thinking *in its application* now begins to determine the apprehension, but is also in turn reciprocally determined in its application by the apprehension (as external impulse) (− B + b), then we say: it is *actually* this or that, regardless of the fact that not the thing, *as thing*, but only our representation of the thing, as a *This* (+ b), has become *actual* in applied thinking; once matters have come that far: then there is no longer any lack of ( − B + b B ), i.e. of the cognizing of the form of the universal in the particular, or of the *necessity* of being represented, as a *This*, with all those sensible multiplicities which our thinking, *as thinking*, previously annihilated on it, i.e. had to transform into a minus of the consciousness of them as *multiplicities*, [p. 227] in order itself to be able to enter into consciousness with a (− B + b) (otherwise no *This*, *as thought*, would have come from it), whereupon those multiplicities can then at once connect themselves again, from the side of animal consciousness, to thinking, or rather first of all to the thought, as thought of a *This* (from − B + b). If it now goes with us with an object of sense, in repeated apprehensions, every time in such a way that the external impulse is on each occasion merely a *conditio sine qua non*, in order at all to awaken the activity of the applied A in us, and the A finds, in the repeated impulses of this object of sense, *for itself* never anything but (B − B), nothing, therefore, whereby it would immediately have to be brought to the thought of a cause under a prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν (to A in − B + b), (aims, purposes): then we call the object *accidental*, i.e. the cause with the prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, which determined it absolutely, stayed away from us in its case (did not move into our consciousness), we do not *comprehend* it, the minus of an animal becoming-conscious with regard to the *extent* of its (sensible) marks takes no hold in its case, and the plus of the narrower drawing-together (greater content) [p. 228] can therefore also not take place, it remains *image*; we must therefore simply put our multiplicator (+ b) into the *image* of it (as we usually do in dreams, and as the child must do with everything at first, when thinking, as we say, begins in it). The (sensible) marks predominate, and no prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν (no aim, no purpose) in *such* an object of sense outside us meets the applied A in us; (no A outside us enters our consciousness with a (− B + b); for no minus of the consciousness of a material *as material* has made room for it). This the ground of the *accidental* in it in our representation, notwithstanding that *in itself* it can be as little accidental as an attribute. I could conclude this remark here, since in the case of the accidental I have come out again at *Kantian* palpability, from which I set out. But the solution of important problems, to which I look forward, determines me to draw also the *inner* impulse into the very same investigation by which the *outer* has been discussed with regard to possibility, actuality, and necessity. With the *inner* impulse, if one compares it with the *outer*, there prevails, namely, the particular difference [p. 229] that it must already, by virtue of animal consciousness, bring with it a minus of the material from which it proceeds, even in the animal; for otherwise no animal consciousness would arise (see above). Here, accordingly, no determinate, sensibly present object for thinking is there; what is there consists in *feeling* (feeling of life). The outer *This* (+ b) therefore drops away here, insofar as what is there consists merely in the *general* feeling of life, just as it drops away in thinking, *as a thinking*, (apart from its application). The possibility of making use of one's thinking with regard to the *inner* impulse will therefore coincide with the possibility of making use of the same with regard to an *outer* impulse insofar as, from within (through feeling, *as feeling*), as from without (through a distant object of sense, — *as distant*), merely the activity of A at all is awakened in an object (B − B); one desires and wills, *what*? indeterminately (whim). But can this be? Does not whim too desire something, only now this, now that? No, it cannot be that one should let it rest at the object, *as object*. (− B), without (under A) in a (+ b) having to retain its form (as B) unalterably [p. 230] (only on a reduced scale) would be a contradiction. A negation drawn to the copula in thinking posits (see above); a negation drawn to the copula in the material posits, and indeed under multiplications (through A). A, once posited in B, describes just as much the act of *its own* application in a (− B), as it describes, in the production of a (+ b) by means of the act of its application to B, the indestructibility of the one form by the other. The one cannot take place at all without the other; (as absurd as it would have been *not* to notice this, just as absurd and disturbing for the analysis it would have been if one had wished to proceed with this and with everything that will now still follow in this remark any sooner; *only now* can the reckoning be concluded, *if it is ever to be carried through to the very last*). Let us therefore assume that as yet no object of sense lies present anywhere in any apprehension; the *conditiones sine quibus non* for the sensibly perceptible of an interaction and manifestation of its essence, to be set up by A, by means of the (B − B + b), in the multiplicity of the material itself, must first present themselves in a certain moment, just now, and assume, [p. 231] what we *must* assume, (A + C), once posited in B, determines *itself*, in this B just as necessarily to a (− B), as it determines this B to a (+ b): then the (A + C), once posited in B, and becoming thereby, in the (− B), *aim, purpose*, must absolutely produce a (+ b), i.e. an *individual* life, (were it only a plant life), i.e. found the *essence* of the same, to which the material will relate partly as sensible attribute, partly as accidens, i.e. on which it will serve partly as *conditio sine qua non*, in order *to fall in this way or that into the senses* as individual, and, according to what it is, to arrive at *this or that* kind of imagining-into the world and imagining-into of the world in it under a consciousness, partly for the affections, without, however, being able to contribute the least to the essence of life, *as of a life in general*, to the essence of consciousness, *as of a consciousness in general*. This paves the way for us still deeper down, *if ever the reckoning is to be carried through to the very last*. Every monad is a mirror of the universe, said the great *Leibniz*, and hid his seer's glance behind images. Every primal material becomes an object (B − B), as soon as an A is posited therein; [p. 232] so runs this apophthegm of his philosophy without picture-language. In every primal material there arises, consequently, an extension, *as extension* (actuality), and a thought, *as thought*, (possibility), *an aim, a purpose*, as soon as an A is posited therein. Now, however, no object can subsist *as mere object*; A, posited in B, presses just as much for the manifestation of its own essence in a (− B), as for the manifestation of the essence (or of the form) on the material in a (+ b); under the stated presupposition, therefore, an organic being, which will be formed in the material itself, *as material*, according to coexistence, affinity, opposition, *must* receive its *essence*. (− B + b) represents accordingly, in the first power, *plant life*, (B, as divisor, remains, and admits no powers), indeed the life of a terrestrial body, according to whose orbit and climatic change the plants describe their orbit. But the A dominates in the life-impulse of a plant over the material only so far as is requisite for an *individual* life-impulse, annihilates only so much material as is unavoidably necessary for the production of an individual life-impulse, without its also making room, through a further annihilation of the material, for a (− B + b) [p. 233] *from outside*, hence for a (B − B² + b²). The plant, that is to say, has neither organs of sense, nor consciousness *through organs of sense*; other beings are not *for it*, hence it too is not *for itself*, but is there for other beings, just as the plant-like *in us* is not there for itself; we experience nothing of *that and how* we grow etc. (see above the *consequentias immediatas*, which have resulted in common for plants and man under ( − B + b B )). Plants are what *Leibniz* calls slumbering monads; they open to man the region of the *beautiful*, and through the beautiful the gateway to the true, in the true to the divine. Unity in the plurality of the form under an A is their design; on this form of the material there hangs again, on the side of the material, its multiplicity, and gives them the wealth of their various coloring. — Where organs of sense are, there they already make room for an object, *as this object*, through the material, in a becoming-conscious; for that reason they are *organs of sense*; although the A itself need not yet, for all that, enter consciousness precisely with its (− B), and organs of sense make room [p. 234] for the object, as a second object outside the *This* of its own: thus there arises from it ( − B² + b² B ). The A dominates here too in the life-impulse, *as life-impulse*, without, however, having itself to enter consciousness with its (− B). The cause, the *individual* life, appears as abandoned by the prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, as mere feeling (instinct) in consciousness. In the lack of a becoming-conscious of the A, with its (− B), although it dominates, *as life-impulse*, in such an individual being, no minus of the becoming-conscious of the quantity and multiplicity of the material in apprehensions and their remnants in the mind can be brought about either; consequently no A with its (− B) can enter consciousness *from outside*, as plus, either; one cannot arrive at any *concepts*, as they were explained above. As the (+ b) from within, (the *individual* life itself) consists in a (+ b) without becoming-conscious of the A, (in instincts), so now also the (+ b) from outside (i.e. the *individual* apprehension together with its remnants) consists in a (+ b) without becoming-conscious of the A under a (− B). *Accidental*, as one thinks, a *This* is held out to one, now here, now there, for [p. 235] his inner impulse, i.e. for the satisfaction of his needs, as they arise as needs in the material; one lives in images, not in concepts, as they were determined above. But the accidental in them lies merely in the lack of a possible entrance of the A as A under a (− B) into animal becoming-conscious, comes from the plus of the quantity and multiplicity of the material in sensible representations, which, as this quantity and multiplicity, was not annihilated by the A dominating merely in the life-impulse, *as life-impulse*; (*animals*, *dreaming* monads; *human beings* in those respects in which they too are *dreaming* monads, i.e. do not know how they come by Something; see above those *consequentias immediatas* which have resulted in common for animal and man under ( − B + b B )). But there prevails here, after all, a significant difference. In the animal there must, to the ( − B + b B ) as its essence, (insofar as it *desires* something) also still, as *essential*, a ( − B + b B ) from outside, if it is to *be able* to represent to itself something in an apprehension, [p. 236] be added; for precisely therein, hence in the ( − B² + b² B ), consists the essence or the possibility of a desiring as well as of a representing. But just as little as the A for that reason has to enter animal consciousness, notwithstanding that it is indeed the prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν for the possibility of a desiring as well as of a representing, just as little does it follow from this that the animal would have to become wholly conscious also of the ( − B + b B ) in an apprehension from outside. According as the plus of the quantity and multiplicity of the material is constituted which is annihilated by A in the becoming-conscious of an external object in the animal, according to that the becoming-conscious of that which that formula asserts *must* be constituted in the animal. Now, however, it is quite well conceivable that in the apprehension of an external object by an animal, there might still remain such a plus of the material in this object, whereby not only the entrance of the A as A, but also of the (− B) into its consciousness would be made impossible. The formula ( − B² + b² B ), which [p. 237] nevertheless grounds the possibility of an animal consciousness, could consequently quite well be admitted in the animal becoming-conscious of the animal itself only with its ( + b² B ); this would mean in words as much as: the animal can, *as animal*, to be sure, in an apprehension be determined through actuality (as the essence of its representations) *under representations* to possibility (as to the essence of its desiring) *under a desiring*, and through possibility *under a desiring* in turn to actuality *under representations*; but it cannot take possibility, *as possibility*, itself into its animal consciousness, cannot *with consciousness* let actuality be determined in itself through possibility, and possibility through actuality, i.e. it cannot *think*. The apprehension is accordingly for the animal too a mere *conditio sine qua non*, notwithstanding that it must appear to it, (as to the man observing the animal), *as completely determining all* its exertions of force, since neither A nor (− B) is admitted into its consciousness; and therefore the ( + b² B ) taken up into it, in the lack [p. 238] of its prius for *animal becoming-conscious*, must concresce with the material straight away for it, which means precisely that it must present itself to it in its phantasy under sheer images with their fillings and multiplicities, as *last*, feigned determinations of its animal activity, (after the manner of the newest German philosophy of intuition and of the saturated *Critical* syllogism of reason). If human beings judge animal economy, they too, as said, usually stop at this or that attribute of the (+ b), hence of the *individual* life-impulse, as supposed cause, without going back to a prius κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, indeed without going back even to a B, *tanquam id, quod est fundamenti loco ponendum* [as that which is to be put in the place of the ground], or to the (+ b) itself, as actuality, determined through possibility; therefore they also usually believe that they can get to the *ground* of animal economy through chemistry, hence through sensibly perceptible experiments which they make with the material, notwithstanding that they might thereby at best arrive at the *conditio sine qua non* for all other conditions, at the *consecutivum essentiae* at the end, after the Orientals and the Greeks began in this matter with water. But it is strange, after all, (what offers itself to me, without being sought [p. 239] by me), that in the essence of the plant only the (+ b) absolutely and in the first power, hence our third *One* as mere multiplicator of extension under A, shows itself absolutely, without our second *One* (extension, as extension, the alteration without further possible alteration) occurring already in the power of plants by means of a (+ b²), i.e. by means of an extension returning to them through organs of sense in the essence of a *representation* for them, (*vis, loco motiva animantium, virtualiter, s. quoad posse data* [the locomotive force of living beings, virtually, or as given in potency]). Should this not entitle me to the heart-lifting thought that the sensations which an object, *as object* (in life *in general*) must have in and for itself, (*virtualiter, s. quoad posse*), together with the sensations which, because (B − B) necessarily produces a (+ b), are necessarily connected with it, thus the sensations of the *father*, of the *mother* and of the *child*, may not be denied to the plant either, notwithstanding that it does not divide them into any representations, does *not enjoy them for itself, as individual*, and yet could enjoy them? — As the plant has indeed feeling, but as yet no representations from outside, so the animal has indeed feeling and representations from outside, but as yet no thoughts; (although [p. 240] its whole existence is determined by aims and purposes, hence by thoughts).

Man describes the ( − B + b B ) of the plant in his feeling of life *as feeling of life*; he describes the ( − B² + b² B ) of the animal in its feelings and representations, *as feelings and representations*; he therefore infallibly has also the ( − B² + b² B ) for his essence. But ( − B² + b² B ) has been found also as the essence of the animal; where, then, would man's proper character be found? Does the A not only dominate, in the individual life-impulse of man, *as life-impulse*, over the change of the material, does it not only promote it there to an alteration without further possible alteration; but also undertake this very alteration without further possible alteration with the sense impressions in general, which have entered according to coexistence, affinity and opposition, as well as remained hanging (concresced) according to this type: then there arises from it such a minus of the becoming-conscious of the quantity and multiplicity [p. 241] (heterogeneity) of the material, in man *also with regard to his representations*, that now even our *first One*, and hence the highest and last plus (the A itself), will be able to enter into animal consciousness, 1 + 1 + 1 = 3 = ( − B³ + b³ B ).

Where it will enter first is easy to judge. Namely there, where it had in any case to dominate wholly over the change of the material, in order merely to bring forth a consciousness, *as consciousness in general* (a *posse* of consciousness by means of the B), *consequently in the feeling of life*, which, *as human feeling of life*, is raised by the entry of the A to personality (− B³ + b³), but not first of all in sensible apprehensions, *as apprehensions*. Man first becomes *person*; there becomes sensible to him immediately, at the hand of Him who bears the universe, *That which is above him, and through him and in him*, the ὄν κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, *the essence of essences*; there becomes audible to him *God’s* voice in *conscience* through the passage of the *Ur-One, of the First, which is and was*, into his *feeling of life*; (without thereby ever being able itself to become *human feeling of life*.) Before his consciousness, however, there passes by, even in the case of [p. 242] sensible *apprehensions*, the formula of the essences, not merely broken off in a ( + b² B ), as with the animal; but it stands there completely inscribed in a ( − B² + b² B ), *even in such cases*, where he is determined, in a sensible apprehension, like the animal, through actuality (as the essence of his representations) *under representations*, to possibility (as to the essence of his desiring) *under a desiring*, and conversely; consequently judges and acts according to *instincts*. The cause (+ b²) then comes to him, as was remarked above of the animal, under mere images with their fillings and multiplicities, yet under an expressly preceding (− B²) in consciousness. For in the human feeling of life itself, *as human*, the third power can indeed never sink so far to the second that man, *as man*, would lose his personality for ever from consciousness. But circumstances can nevertheless occur under which *temporarily* the quantity and multiplicity of the material, entering through apprehensions or stirring in any case in animal consciousness, becomes so overwhelming that the feeling of life (as happens in [p. 243] dreams) sinks to the second power. A coagulation of images, which lies before him, and which with its mere existence, according to coexistence, affinity and opposition, dupes him as an actual object, now hinders man from taking possibility, *as possibility*, into his consciousness without the animal appendage of conditions merely simulating it; he can no longer with consciousness let actuality be determined in himself through possibility, nor possibility through actuality, he no longer thinks in this moment, the A is lacking to him, his Ergo is no Ergo any more, hardly does the (+ b²), now set in mere images, maintain, through a (− B²) that always precedes it in human consciousness, i.e. through dreamt-up possibilities in those images themselves and through fabling projects, a weak connection with the A that has stepped out of consciousness; man is no longer man.—The object *in him*, and the object *outside him* through the object, *as object*, as it is given to his human becoming-conscious full and unmutilated, to let be determined under A, as the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, (setting aside all conditions already simulating beforehand this and that, which have to attach themselves to it only afterwards); and, because (B − B) [p. 244] necessarily produces a (+ b), by means of the (− B³), raised to the determining power through his representations as well as feelings, (*insofar as he wants to be called man*), also to found a (+ b³), i.e. a life corresponding to that first one, this is his great task, *in order to be man*. Unpardonable therefore is the unphilosophy which, with the dreamt-up inadmissibility of a physico-theology, would turn into a *deception* for us the will of God that meets man in every plant, since precisely this is the most real thing, and, for the weakness of our character, which even through supposed culture is ever more abandoned to animality, the most essential need. Even the doctrine of fables has, in the mouth of a philosopher to whom the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν was already previously revealed, and who therefore regards the images as means, not as end, more sense than such a philosophy which, under the name of a war of extermination against *dogmatism*, fights with images, *as if they were end* (*or: thing in itself*), consequently with windmills. When the dying Socrates, *as Socrates*, speaks these last words of his life to *Crito*: *Friend, do not forget to bring a cock to the god of healing; for we owe him one*: [p. 245] so he is herein more a philosopher, i.e. *man*, than the German who in a great thick book ventures to impute to thinking itself, *as a thinking*, the deceptive property of phantasy, (a thought that destroys itself; for the man had after all to represent to himself *in his own head* even this non-empirical dogma as truth) in order, by a *dialectically* made thinking, *as thinking*, to annihilate phantasy-images, by working up through which (as through mere means) to the cognition of the essence of essences (as to the end) lies mankind’s divine vocation. No, even *skepticism* does not do this, and if it ever does or did do it: then it would deserve no refutation; *for it would destroy itself*. Yet without the course of speculation, hitherto left behind, in relation to the daily tasks of our conscience, (of thinking, as it reveals itself in our feeling of life), the sophistications of a critique of pure reason would not have been possible at all. *Actuality as actuality* to let be determined through possibility, *as possibility*, under an A in general, and hence an essence of essences, as primal being for everything that is, to find *apodictically*, (*omnibus, ex nihilo ducendis, sufficit Unum* [for drawing all things out of nothing, One suffices] *Leibniz*), and then, however, [p. 246] downward into life and into the world, to let possibility *in its application* (as − B) also again reciprocally be determined by actuality, in the necessary (by the absolute will of God as the primal being indeed absolutely, but also under ends, hence with *wisdom* dependent) production of a (+ b), and hence to assume no manifestation of the A *in us* without a (+ C), without preceding apprehensions, no manifestation of the same *for us* without coexistence, affinity, opposition in the material, (on which actuality lies as ground precisely *as their essence*), in short, to let the object *in us* and the object *outside us*, through the object, *as object*, as it is given to our human becoming-conscious full and unmutilated, be determined under A as the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, (setting aside all conditions already simulating beforehand this and that, which have to attach themselves to it only afterwards), this is just as much the misjudged *goal of all speculation* as it furnished above, in what is essential, the foundation of our conscience. Without many difficulties the following propositions might now arise from this also as the foundation of all speculation. Under a *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν everything that is is determined through possibility and actuality, with wisdom [p. 247] and necessity, of which the former, (possibility, namely), refers back to the essence of the primal being, the latter, (actuality, namely), to the essence of the material. Nothing can therefore be actual that is not possible; otherwise its actuality would be a contradiction, and nothing can be possible (− B i.e. determined wisely and necessarily by the application of an A to it, according to purposes) that is not actual; otherwise its possibility would be a contradiction. *This* possibility and actuality, however, to apply further to the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν as well, to the primal being itself, and thereby to want to determine the same, since they are after all first posited through it, would likewise be a contradiction, and would mean as much as making the creator into the creature. To the Unnameable, Unchangeable, All-wise, Almighty there belongs only a *being* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, an ὄν κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, but no longer the character of the standard of measure according to which it guides the course of the universe, and on which it bound, on this side the universe itself, on the other side its own being in a *human* becoming-conscious. Notwithstanding that this possibility and actuality constitutes our own essence, like the essence of all that is (i.e. that has an existence through *that being*): it yet enters only gradually into our *individual* [p. 248] consciousness under the *conditionibus* of affinity, coexistence, attraction and repulsion in the material, *as material*, from without, and under the corresponding *conditionibus* of similarity, coexistence and opposition in the material, *as material*, from within, (*Association*), which, considered as from within (as the inherited legacy of our dear I, as Ours), compose or configure our animal consciousness *individually*; but do not found its possibility, or its essence itself, (not even as *individually possible*). For since through affinity from without and similarity from within, through coexistence from without and within, through attraction together with repulsion from without and opposition from within, as through the material, separated from its essence (considered as non-ground, non-cause, least of all as *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, but as a mere *conditio sine qua non* for *us children of men*), and hence through apprehensions from without and corresponding individual becoming-conscious from within, *thinking* in us is stirred up, while apprehensions, *as apprehensions*, and an individual becoming-conscious, *as individual*, absolutely cannot be changed into any thinking: thus thinking in us annihilates everything in the material or in the [p. 249] apprehensions, *as apprehensions*, in the individual becoming-conscious, *as individual*, that is material, and hence non-ground, non-cause, non-*prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, and hence mere affinity from without and similarity from within, coexistence from without and within, attraction together with repulsion from without and opposition from within, and brings it in this to the *object*, to possibility and actuality, to the form of thinking and to the form of the material (B − B), but also to the object as a *This*, to actuality determined through possibility (+ b); hence it brings thinking, in an individual human consciousness, to the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, to the ground and to the cause. But these three components depend on apprehensions on the one side, on an individual becoming-conscious, *as individual*, on the other. They are that which cannot be changed into an apprehension *as apprehension*, but for that very reason can be aroused through it; they are that which cannot be changed into an individual becoming-conscious, *as individual*, but for that very reason can be aroused through it. The excitability of the cause (i.e. of actuality determined through possibility) in our *individual* consciousness depends on the affinity with regard to the material from without, on the corresponding [p. 250] similarity with regard to the material from within (*called association through similarity*); the one as much as the other is *medius* for the excitability of the cause in our individual becoming-conscious, whereby one proceeds from sensible accidents to sensible attributes, in order to obtain *rationes sufficientes?* [sufficient reasons?] in the doctrine of the gods, as the *rabble* understood it; in our physics *forces* (without − B), and in the *Critique syllogisms of reason*. The excitability of the *ground* (*ejus, quod est Fundamenti loco ponendum* [of that which is to be put in the place of the ground], of actuality, or of extension, *as extension*) in our *individual* becoming-conscious depends on the coexistence in the material, outwardly as well as inwardly (as on *their* medius), without similarity in certain multiplicities stepping in between and separating the coexistence in consciousness. Hence B as B, stirred up in the coexistence without multiplicity stepping in between in consciousness, is answered, by thinking, through a necessary positing of thinking in the same as B, (space, time, mathematics), but by the impulse of life through the feeling of the *sublime*, i.e. through a momentary extension of the + b (of the cause in us) into the foundation of its actuality = B by means of thinking in us = A. One should not forget to remark that already a [p. 251] minus of becoming-conscious of *similarity* in *multiplicities* must enter, in order to make room for the plus of coexistence *as coexistence*. The excitability of the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν (of that possibility which no longer requires any possibility for the determination of its being, hence of the absolute necessity in being κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν), in our *individual* becoming-conscious depends on the attraction together with repulsion with regard to the material from without, the corresponding opposition with regard to the material from within, (*association through opposition* so called); the one as much as the other is *medius* (condition) of the excitability of the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν in our individual becoming-conscious. Here, then, a minus of becoming-conscious of similarity in multiplicities, a minus of becoming-conscious of affinity in the material, *as material outside us*, must enter, in order to make room for the plus of coexistence, *as a coexistence* in the phenomenon of attraction. But the plus of coexistence shows here, in its train, again a minus of the same in repulsion (or in opposition, *as an opposition with what was previously posited*). Every approach to the One is removal from the Other in the world; every movement [p. 252] is an alteration falling within the senses, which presents to us an approach to the One and removal from the Other in the world, hence attraction and repulsion as phenomenon, or, which is one thing, the alternating alteration in coexistence, *sensibly*, and consequently brings a plus and at the same time a minus into our consciousness. But to every minus a plus must again correspond. A position of coexistence, and in this position of a coexistence in turn a non-position of coexistence, cannot take place absolutely without a Third, grounding the position as well as the non-position; for how should a position be able at the same time to ground its own non-position? Here, then, there *must* be assumed either a *contingentia* (*Epicurean* chance, *Kantian* freedom, *Reinholdian* arbitrary will, *Fichtean*, all-positing act, *as act*,—*Fortuna volubilis errat!* [Fickle Fortune strays!])—i.e. a world-system that begins with a minus, and consequently without a head, or there *must* be assumed a *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, a *primus motor*, an unchangeable primal ground of all motion. One has no other choice; either a not-thinking in a *contingentia*, or a thinking in an absolute necessity, (at whose side stands wisdom), by which also this apparent contingency [p. 253] must be determined. In such circumstances, since the cause, the ground, the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν becomes excitable in our consciousness only under the conditions named, it easily happens that we take the conditions themselves, hence the affinity (similarity), coexistence, attraction together with repulsion, for the cause, the ground, the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, (so that to us, hence, what is the affair of the A is concresced with C), instead of having to presuppose the cause, the ground, the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, in order thereby to make an affinity, coexistence, attraction together with repulsion first possible, and to let these last three components attach themselves, with the *possibility* of their existence, to the formula of the essences ( − B + b B ). The physicist, e.g., will be able to believe that he has found out the cause of a thunderstorm in *electricity*, which a machine presents to him, whereas this electricity itself is nothing other than the sensible utterance of what follows necessarily from actuality, determined through possibility, given presupposed affinity of the material, hence a (+ b), given under certain *conditionibus* of the material, in which the mythologist makes known his confused insight into the necessary determination of the [p. 254] actuality *through possibility* (under the image of a *Jupiter tonans*), the physicist on the other hand makes known his clear insight into these very *conditiones*, under which a necessary determination of actuality through possibility manifests itself sensibly in a determinate way; for the physicist himself will not deny that he can see no cause; but certainly that under certain conditions he can let the concept of a cause, which *as cause* will always be the same, be aroused in himself. (When I speak of the cause as a *concept*, I understand, as is easily judged, the word *concept* not as it has hitherto been commonly explained; but take it for the *possibility* of a complexion of sensible apprehensions, in short for + b; I do not like to make new words; the only new word that I remember having made is the above one of a brilliant *judger* [*Urtheiler*]: whoever dislikes this may put instead: brilliant *raisonneur*; then it is quite French.) The mathematician will be able to believe that he has found the ground of the *equality* of the one angle, (so he says), at the base of an isosceles △ with the other, in the coexistence of the lines of this △, although this coexistence under an affinity is only the condition under which [p. 255] he recognizes the necessary form of the universal also in the form of the particular *as necessary*. The coexistence of the sun with the day, often repeated in our apprehension, makes us recognize it as a *conditio sine qua non* for the coming of day, and makes it therefore stir up the concept of cause in us, although it, *as something palpable to the senses*, can be anything but a cause, and although from that presupposition of an often repeated coexistence in our becoming-conscious, as the grounding of a cause, it would follow that a comet, with whose appearance great world-events coexist every time, must also contain the cause of them; just as little as the letters are the cause of what we read or write, so little is the sun, *as something palpable to the senses*, cause of the day.—Even when one has written a critique of pure reason, one will be able to believe that attraction together with repulsion is the *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν itself;—but no,—the *opposition*, the *either—or* of the disjunctive syllogism is said to lead to a *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, but this *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν is said to be dialectical, until one hastens to its aid practically. This *opposition*, this *either—or* we must here still hold fast for just so [p. 256] long, until we thereby also fully arrive at the end-point of the *accidental*; for the *essence* of things we have. What in our cognition is the last, the pure cognition of a *prius* κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν, that is in the essence of things themselves the first, is the essence of essences, is *A as A in A through A*. In it the possibility (− B) of all that receives an existence through that being is given. It is unchangeable, and beyond every *either—or*, as beyond every *hypothesis*. But it has a material before it, or creates for itself a material; this is indifferent to philosophy; for the *creating of a material*, as creating of a *material*, falls into a region where it can absolutely determine nothing more *through itself* that is *act*, not *being*. It knows only, and knows *apodictically*, that with the A, *as A, in A, through A*, there must also be assumed a material = C, hence some coexistence or other, let it come from where it will, as unavoidable *hypothesis* (substrate, presupposition) *for actuality*, although it comes to fruition as actuality itself only through the application of the A to this coexistence with it; for just as little as the (B − B) can already be applied to A as A, just as little can, as is easily judged, this (B − B) already be applied to C [p. 257] *as C*; (the nought is no contradiction, but it also signifies nothing without a preceding *one*). Attraction together with repulsion, alternating alteration in coexistence, movement, was what led us last of all to A as necessary *primus motor*. Attraction together with repulsion, alternating alteration in coexistence, movement, will therefore again have to be the first thing that goes on, through the application of A to C, in C, and through which this C is advanced to an alteration without further possible alteration, to extension, *as extension*, to actuality after preceding possibility, to the beginning of a system of things according to purposes, to the (B − B). A system of things *according to purposes*, however, it cannot be, unless everything that is possible through (− B) in its connection with B thereby also becomes actual, hence (− B + b). Affinities will therefore *have to* present themselves, to this end, for the organism, and can present themselves by virtue of the preceding attraction and repulsion, by virtue of the preceding alternating alteration in coexistence; although they, as little as attraction and repulsion *for themselves*, can *found* the least thing in the organism, *as organism*. In order [p. 258] therefore to find mechanism, as it *now* is, comprehensibly, one must absolutely presuppose the organism as its ground. But in order to be able to take up the organism into the phenomena in which it now expresses itself, *as phenomena*, one must, besides the A, already originally also posit a C, for the purpose of the formula of the essences ( − B + b B ) unavoidably; whether this necessary coexistence has likewise arisen through the A, for which an attested fact speaks, or has not arisen, which philosophy, *as philosophy*, must leave undecided. Everything that now has an existence has its existence through ( − B + b B ) under an (A + C), so much is indisputable; indisputable, therefore, is it also that everything that now falls under our senses even *as mere mechanism* presupposes that formula. In other words: the necessary hypothesis for the possibility of the ( − B + b B ), namely the coexistence of the A with C in (A + C), is at the same time the necessary hypothesis of all existence, as it now falls everywhere into the senses; a material *must* be assumed, which [p. 259] through its existence leads first to the *being of the second order*, i.e. to ( − B + b B ) under an A, as the ὄν κατ᾽ ἐξοχήν; but now also through the *being of the second order*, i.e. through ( − B + b B ) under an A is so completely determined that all its change in all parts of the immeasurable realm of God, ordered and governed by God, under a ( − B + b B ), *as its essence*, *must run its course*. *Under a* ( − B + b B ) *as its essence*, it must run its course; but it could surely no longer be *its essence*, if the material had lost, through the annihilation of *it* by means of the A down to its form, also its *existence*, its, now regulated, coexistence with A. There is thus once more in this world of God no absolute annihilation; (*Kant’s a priori synthetic?* proposition that in all alterations of the corporeal world the *quantity of matter* remains unaltered), as where a *copula* would be annihilated, and this cannot be anyway. Even the relative annihilation of the material under A is rebirth [p. 260] of the same under a rule ( − B + b B ), is alternating transposition of its existence with a being, is all that it can ever gain. If the material remains, and it does remain: then a change remains; but the material remains now *under a rule*, hence a change remains under a rule. If a change under a rule remains, then the rule, *as rule*, must determine the change, but also the change, *as under a rule*, must determine the rule *in its application*. If the change, *as under a rule*, determines the rule, which it did not give but received, *in its application*: then this can again mean as much as: it determines it *to* its application, and then the change under a rule will, through *apprehensions* (as impulses), stir up the rule itself, *as rule*, hence as ( − B + b B ) in a consciousness such as the human one, e.g., is, i.e. precisely: awaken it in turn in a change under a rule, according to the multiplicities of coexistence, similarity and opposition of the material; or it can mean as much as: the change under a rule determines the rule quite properly *in* its application, i.e. this rule, which [p. 261] does not let itself be altered, will also *have to manifest in itself* the existence of the change, *hence the existence of the material itself*, as it is constituted on each occasion in its multiplicities, according to the measure of coexistence, of attraction together with repulsion, and of affinity, (notwithstanding that we invert it, and say, the rule manifests itself in the material, whereas the existence of the material manifests itself rather in that rule and according to it.) If the change under a rule determines the rule quite properly *in* its application: there arises an organic being ( − B + b B ). If the change under a rule determines the rule, already *in* its application manifested, also still *to* its application: there arises an organic being with a *faculty of representation* ( − B<sup>2</sup> + b<sup>2</sup> B ). If finally the rule, already determined in its application through the change, and also still advanced *to* its application in a faculty of representation, determines the change itself, as it runs its course under a rule: there arises a *thinking* organic being ( − B<sup>3</sup> + b<sup>3</sup> B ), *waking* monad. This [p. 262] monad will *wake*, insofar as it determines the change itself, as it runs its course under a rule, *through the rule*. This monad will *slumber*, insofar as the change under a rule determines the rule quite properly *in* its application in it (feeling in an *individuality*). This monad will *dream*, insofar as the change under a rule determines the rule, already manifested *in* its application, also still *to* its application in it (faculty of representation in an *individuality*).
